# PART B: MONEY

Part B freezes the rules the prototype has today (Harsh's ruling) and fixes only what QA proved to be a plain bug. Each rule cites the line in the prototype it comes from. Where a rule is kept but may be wrong for Siddhi's business, it is flagged and listed in section 16. Where draft 2 goes beyond "as coded" (per-line rounding, refund, re-issue, advance), it says so and lists it as an open item.

## 8. Pricing rules

Symbols for one bill line: `NW` customer net weight (g); `q` quantity; `r` metal rate or piece price; `l` labour rate; `p` piroi; `o` other charges. All amounts are exact decimals (S3); "round" means round half up to the stated number of decimals.

- **R1. Labour rate by sale type.** Three different chains exist in the prototype and each has its own place. (1) **At stock creation**, a blank semi-wholesale rate takes the wholesale rate, else the retail rate, and a blank wholesale rate takes the retail rate (`AddDesignClient.tsx:1124-1127`); in the schema the three rates are never blank. (2) **At billing**, the default `l` is the piece's `labour_retail`, `labour_semi` or `labour_wholesale` for the bill's sale type (as coded `BillingPOSClient.tsx:28-32`: Wholesale falls back to semi, then retail, then the old single `labour`; Semi-Wholesale to semi, wholesale, retail, then `labour`; this default is applied by the screen, and the API prices the labour rate it is sent). The line's `l` can be edited on the screen. (3) **For old data** (the import), a missing semi-wholesale rate is `round((retail + wholesale) / 2, 2)` (`designStore.ts:45`). *Test:* T-A02, T-A03, T-C16. *Source:* AC1, F21.
- **R2. Default metal rate.** For a By Weight piece the screen pre-fills `r` with the `daily_rates` rate for the piece's material and purity that has the latest `rate_date` not after the bill's date; for a Per Piece piece `r` is the piece price for the sale type (R1) (the code reads one settings rate per material, `BillingPOSClient.tsx:98-100`; the by-purity, by-date default is the fix for QA BIL-10). `r` is **always sent by the screen** and is required by X1; it can be edited on the line. If no daily rate exists for the material and purity (for example `Custom`, 20K or 925 with no row), the screen asks for one and X1 returns the warning `RATE_MISSING`; if the latest rate is dated before the bill's date it returns `RATE_STALE`. *Test:* T-A13, T-A17. *Source:* BIL-10, F14.
- **R3. Labour amount.** By Weight: `round(NW × l, 2)`. Per Piece: `0`, because the labour is inside the piece price (as coded, `BillingPOSClient.tsx:34-39`, shown as "Included in piece price", `BillingItemsTable.tsx:144`). *Test:* T-A01, T-A06.
- **R4. Fine due.** `NW × q` when the sale type is **Wholesale** and the material is **Silver** and the selling method is **By Weight**; otherwise 0. *Fix:* the prototype also applied it to Per Piece pieces, which set the metal amount to 0 and dropped the piece price (`BillingPOSClient.tsx:41-44`, QA BIL-06). Purity and wastage are not applied (kept as coded, O2). *Test:* T-A04, T-A05. *Source:* BIL-06, BIL-07.
- **R5. Metal amount.** If the line's fine due is greater than 0: `0` (the silver is settled later, SF1). Else By Weight: `round(NW × r, 2)`; Per Piece: `round(r × q, 2)`. *Test:* T-A01, T-A04, T-A06. *Source:* `BillingPOSClient.tsx:46-51`.
- **R6. Quantity.** An integer from 1 to 1,000. A By Weight piece must have `q = 1` (the screen shows "1" for them, `BillingItemsTable.tsx:99`; O13). The quantity is saved on the line. *Fix:* the prototype lost it on save and every edit cut the price to one piece (`BillingSummaryPanel.tsx:95-115`, QA BIL-05). *Test:* T-A06. *Source:* BIL-05.
- **R7. Line total.** `metal_amount + labour_amount + p + o`. Piroi and other charges are added once per line, not multiplied by `q` (as coded, `BillingPOSClient.tsx:53-55`; O9). **Per-line rounding to paise (R3, R5) is new:** the prototype keeps floating-point fractions until it rounds the grand total, and a simulation of 200,000 random bills found that this rule changes the grand total by ₹1 on about 0.8 percent of bills. It is kept because exact paise on every line is what the ledger, the invariants and any later GST invoice need; it is flagged for Harsh as a ruling (O22). *Test:* T-A01, T-A07, T-A19. *Source:* F8, AC4.
- **R8. Bill totals.** `subtotal = sum of line totals`. `discount >= 0` and `additional_charges >= 0`. `before_round = subtotal - discount + additional_charges`, and **`before_round` must be at least 0**: a discount that would make it negative is rejected (a discount equal to `subtotal + additional_charges` is allowed and gives grand total 0). GST is 0 (estimate, as coded). `grand_total = round(before_round, 0)`, always a whole rupee (as coded: `Math.round` is applied whether or not the round-off setting is on, `BillingSummaryPanel.tsx:149`). `round_off = grand_total - before_round` when `settings.estimate.roundOff` is on, else `0`; it can be negative (1,000.40 gives −0.40). The prototype shows the Round Off line only when the setting is on (O12). *Test:* T-A08, T-A10. *Source:* DAT-10, BIL-04, `appStore.ts:253-275`, F8.
- **R9. Sale type.** Choosing a customer sets the bill's sale type to the customer's type (as coded, `BillingPOSClient.tsx:158-161`); the biller may change it. The sale type is stored on the bill and never changes (no edit in M1). *Test:* T-A03.
- **R10. Pieces that may be billed.** `In Stock` pieces; `Reserved` and `On Approval` pieces only when the screen passes their ids in `p_confirm_held` after showing a warning; `Sold`, `Returned`, `Repair`, `Transferred`, `Missing`, `Damaged` and `Archived` pieces never. *Fix:* the prototype only blocked `Sold` (`BillingPOSClient.tsx:85`, QA BIL-11). *Test:* T-C08. *Source:* BIL-11, O7.
- **R11. Payment status** (derived, never stored): `Cancelled` if the bill is cancelled; else `Paid` when pending is 0 and fine pending is 0; `Partial` when received is greater than 0 or fine settled is greater than 0; else `Pending` (as coded, `appStore.ts:62-63`). *Test:* T-B02.
- **R12. One function.** These rules are implemented once, in `priceLine` and `priceBill` of `pricing.ts`. `quote_bill` (X7) and `create_bill` (X1) call the same code, so a quote and a saved bill cannot differ. The same file runs in the browser (C12), and it is tested against an independent exact-decimal oracle on 200 random inputs. *Test:* T-A14, T-A15. *Source:* hazard 5.
- **R13. Versions.** A bill stores `pricing_version` (1 for the rules in this section). A later change to any rule (the fine formula, the piroi unit, rounding) becomes version 2 with its own functions; old bills keep version 1, and `quote_bill`, the nightly re-pricing check (I6) and the tests take the version as a parameter. So a rule change never breaks the check on old bills. *Test:* T-A15 (one case per version). *Source:* F11.
- **R14. List prices.** For every line the server records the price the screen should have offered, **computed by the server and not taken from the client**: `list_metal_rate` (the daily rate by R2, or the piece price) and `list_labour_rate` (the piece's rate for the sale type; a Per Piece line is compared on the price only, because its `labour_rate` is unused, R3). A line sold below either, or a discount above 10 percent of the subtotal, is counted in the nightly report to the owner (I11); nothing is blocked (O26). *Test:* T-B24, T-A13. *Source:* F12.

Worked example (the QA harness's own figure): NW 35.42 g, rate 6,920, labour 353 per gram, piroi 2.15, Retail, By Weight: metal 245,106.40, labour 12,503.26, line total 257,611.81, subtotal 257,611.81, grand total **257,612** (QA T2: ₹2,57,612). *Test:* T-A01.

## 9. Bills, receipts, allocation and fine

### 9.1 Life cycle

- **K1.** A bill is created `active` and can only become `cancelled`, once, by `cancel_bill` or `reissue_bill` (owner). There is **no edit** in M1: the bill and its lines are read-only. A correction is a **re-issue** (X10): the wrong bill is cancelled and the corrected bill is saved in one step, and the money already received moves to the corrected bill. Editing a bill with its payments intact is M2 (section 17). *Test:* T-D08, T-B19. *Source:* BIL-09, DAT-02, DAT-03, CMP-09, `PLAN.md` M1 cut, F2.
- **K2.** Creating a bill marks every piece on it `Sold` and sets its `sold_bill_id`, in the same transaction, only if each piece is still billable (R10). Two billers saving the same piece at the same moment: exactly one succeeds, the other gets `JV_PIECE_UNAVAILABLE` naming the piece, and **no number is used and nothing else changes**. *Test:* T-C04, T-C06. *Source:* INV-01, BIL-02, docs #1, #5.
- **K3.** Cancelling a bill (X5, with no replacement, for example when the customer returns the goods): the bill becomes `cancelled` with reason, time, business date (`cancelled_on`, D11) and person; each of its pieces whose `sold_bill_id` is this bill returns to `In Stock`; the bill's allocations get `released_at`; the **receipts stay active** (the money was received), so the customer holds an advance, which Y6 applies to their other pending bills or leaves as credit. Handing the money back is X11. *Test:* T-B10, T-B20. *Source:* BIL-12.
- **K4.** A customer is never deleted. They can be archived only when their outstanding, fine outstanding, advance and fine advance are all zero. Their bills and receipts stay. *Test:* T-C02. *Source:* LED-03, DAT-01.
- **K5.** The walk-in customer exists once, is never archived and never holds a PAN. Its id is the fixed uuid `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001` (name "Walk-in Customer", no mobile), written by the seed, so that the import and the tests can name it. It may carry dues like anyone else (as coded: the prototype used the text id `walk-in`, `BillingSummaryPanel.tsx:137`), and its dues count in the outstanding figures; because one row stands for many different people, a sale that is not fully paid to the walk-in is **allowed but flagged** (O6), cash at the limit and bills at the PAN threshold need a named customer (section 13). **The walk-in never holds an advance**, because one row stands for strangers whose money must not pay each other's bills: X2 for the walk-in must name the bill (`p_bill_id`, so a payment never goes to another stranger's older bill) and takes no more than that bill's pending (`JV_OVERPAYMENT`), X5 refuses a walk-in bill that has live allocations with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` (its receipts are voided first, with the reason, which shows the money going back on that day), X10 refuses a surplus of money over the new bill's total **and a surplus of silver grams over its fine due** the same way (a re-issue from Wholesale to Retail, or with fewer grams, would otherwise leave silver credit on the walk-in row, which I10 forbids; a review run of 450 random sequences stopped on exactly this 28 times, and with the silver check added none of 450 did, RM4-1), X3 for the walk-in must name its bill too and settles no more grams than that bill's fine pending (`JV_FINE_EXCEEDS`; O52, default yes), X12 refuses an advance for it, and I10 checks it. *Test:* T-B17, T-B23. *Source:* LED-07, F9, RM-3; RM4-1, RM4-16.
- **K6.** A re-issued bill points back to the one it replaces (`reissue_of`), a cancelled bill can be re-issued only once, and the customer cannot change in a re-issue; a second re-issue of the same bill meets a cancelled bill and raises `JV_BILL_CANCELLED`. *Test:* T-B19. *Source:* F2.

### 9.2 Receipts and allocation

- **Y1. Receipts.** Money received is a `receipts` row of kind `money` (mode Cash, UPI, Card, Bank Transfer or Other). Silver received is kind `fine_silver`. Silver settled in cash at a day's rate is kind `fine_cash` with `amount = round(grams × rate, 2)`; that amount **is money received** (cash totals include it) but it reduces fine, not rupees owed (as coded the cash was not booked at all, `appStore.ts:441-455`, QA LED-11). Money handed back is kind `refund` (Y10). Whether silver settled in cash counts as a sale or a receipt for tax is a question for Siddhi's CA `[CA]` (O2). *Test:* T-B12, T-B20. *Source:* LED-11, CMP-06, DAT-14.
- **Y2. "Credit" is not a payment.** A bill left partly or fully unpaid simply has no receipt for the rest. The mode `Credit` does not exist (D13). *Test:* T-B01. *Source:* BIL-03.
- **Y3. Oldest first.** To apply an amount (or fine grams) to bills, take the customer's `active` bills with something pending, ordered by `bill_date`, then `created_at`, then `id`. If the caller names a bill, it must belong to the customer, be active and have something pending (otherwise `JV_INVALID_INPUT`), and it goes first. Each bill takes the smaller of what remains and what is pending. "Something pending" means money pending for a money payment (X2) and silver pending for a fine settlement (X3); a payment row of amount 0 (the screen's default row) is ignored; a `fine_cash` settlement whose amount `round(grams × rate, 2)` is 0.00 raises `JV_INVALID_INPUT`. *Fix:* the prototype's "Apply to oldest outstanding" applied the payment to nothing (`recordPayment` with no invoice id, `appStore.ts:350-397`, QA LED-04). *Test:* T-B04, T-B05, T-B22. *Source:* LED-04, DAT-08.
- **Y4. Same customer only.** An allocation can only join a receipt and a bill of the same customer, enforced by composite foreign keys (D14) and again in the functions. *Test:* T-B06. *Source:* LED-01.
- **Y5. Overpayment.** At bill creation, payments totalling more than the bill's grand total are rejected (`JV_OVERPAYMENT`). In `record_payment`, any amount beyond what the customer's bills need is kept as an **advance** (money received that is neither allocated to a bill nor handed back) and the result says so; it is never lost. *Test:* T-B03, T-B04. *Source:* LED-06.
- **Y6. Advance and rebalance.** After any function that changes a customer's bills or receipts, the customer's **advance** is applied to their oldest pending bills, oldest receipt first (by `paid_on`, then `created_at`, then `id`), and unallocated silver grams the same way. **A rebalance applies in total at most the customer's `advance` of V2** (money received − live allocations − refunds), so money that was handed back is never spent a second time; opening advances are money received like any other. This **never changes an existing allocation**. Afterwards a customer has an advance only if they have no pending bill (invariant I10). Applying an advance to a new bill is shown on the billing screen as "Advance adjusted: ₹X" (O23). *Test:* T-B07, T-B14, T-B20, T-B22, T-B26. *Source:* review Q5, F1, RM-1.
- **Y7. Void, not delete.** `void_receipt` (owner) marks a receipt `voided` with a reason; its allocations stop counting; the receipt and the void stay in the ledger. Balances are derived, so a void restores exactly what the receipt had applied. A void shows in the ledger and in the daily totals **on the day it is done** (`voided_on`, D13), as a reversing line (V3, V4), so a closed day's cash total never changes because of it; the owner may void a receipt of an earlier day (a back-dated receipt or refund by the owner, Y8, does change an earlier day: O3). A void is refused with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` ("void the refund first") when the customer's advance after it would be below 0, which happens only when the money was already handed back (X4). *Fix:* the prototype deleted the entry and could lose fine silver (`appStore.ts:462-518`, QA LED-02). *Test:* T-B08, T-B09. *Source:* LED-02, F2.
- **Y8. Dates.** `paid_on` and `bill_date` default to today (Asia/Kolkata). A biller can only use today; the owner can use any date up to and including today; a future date is rejected. A bill's date cannot be changed afterwards (the prototype let it be re-dated with no log, `updateInvoiceDate`). *Test:* T-B16. *Source:* CMP-09, LED-08.
- **Y9. Rebalance covers bills of every kind**, including `opening_balance` bills, which are the oldest by construction (D15). *Test:* T-B05, T-B21.
- **Y10. Refund.** `issue_refund` (owner, X11) records money handed back: kind `refund`, an amount greater than 0 and not more than the customer's current advance, a mode, and a date by Y8. It has no allocations, reduces the advance, appears in the ledger as a debit and in the daily totals as money paid out on its own date. Voiding a refund (X4) restores the advance. How a refund of money received against an estimate is treated for tax is a question for Siddhi's CA `[CA]` (O24). *Test:* T-B20. *Source:* F2.

### 9.3 Silver fine

- **SF1.** Fine is the silver weight owed from wholesale silver By Weight lines (R4). It is settled later by `settle_fine` in silver (`fine_silver`) or in cash at that day's silver rate (`fine_cash`, rate required and greater than 0). *Test:* T-B11, T-B13. *Source:* `appStore.ts:399-460`.
- **SF2.** Grams are applied oldest first (Y3), the named bill first. The grams must be greater than 0 and **not more than the customer's fine outstanding**; otherwise `JV_FINE_EXCEEDS` with the maximum allowed. *Fix:* the prototype capped silently and lost the extra silver (QA LED-12). *Test:* T-B11. *Source:* LED-12.
- **SF3.** Fine is not adjusted for purity, touch or wastage (as coded, QA BIL-07, LED-13, CMP-06). `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION: how is fine worked out on Siddhi's bills? Siddhi and her CA, default: net weight, as coded]` (O2). A change becomes pricing version 2 (R13). *Test:* T-A04. *Source:* BIL-07.

## 10. The functions

All callable functions are service functions of the API (S12), reached by the routes below. Each begins with the role check (A6), is the only door to what it writes, and runs inside one transaction. Each function that writes is repeat-safe through the `Idempotency-Key` header (L5) and returns JSON holding every row it created or changed so the cache can apply them (C5(b)). All raise `JV_*` codes (S8). **Input names keep draft 3's `p_` prefix so that reviews stay traceable; in the request body the prefix is dropped (`p_customer_id` is `customer_id`), money and weight fields are strings (S3), and `p_request_id` is the `Idempotency-Key` header.** A function that "raises" answers with the HTTP status of S8.

| Function | Route | Success | Owner | Biller | Staff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 `create_bill` | `POST /v1/bills` | 201 | yes | yes | no |
| X2 `record_payment` | `POST /v1/customers/{id}/payments` | 201 | yes | yes | no |
| X3 `settle_fine` | `POST /v1/customers/{id}/fine-settlements` | 201 | yes | yes | no |
| X4 `void_receipt` | `POST /v1/receipts/{id}/void` | 200 | yes | no | no |
| X5 `cancel_bill` | `POST /v1/bills/{id}/cancel` | 200 | yes | no | no |
| X6 `create_customer` | `POST /v1/customers` | 201 | yes | yes | no |
| X7 `quote_bill` | `POST /v1/bills/quote` | 200 | yes | yes | no |
| X8 `create_design` | `POST /v1/designs` | 201 | yes (may send `original_net_weight`) | no | yes (sending it gives `JV_FORBIDDEN`) |
| X9 `set_sku_status` | `POST /v1/skus/status` | 200 | yes | no | yes |
| X10 `reissue_bill` | `POST /v1/bills/{id}/reissue` | 201 | yes | no | no |
| X11 `issue_refund` | `POST /v1/customers/{id}/refunds` | 201 | yes | no | no |
| X12 `add_opening_balance` | `POST /v1/customers/{id}/opening-balance` | 201 | yes | no | no |

Field rules that belong to the same matrix and are tested the same way: a biller may use only today's date (Y8), so a past `p_bill_date` or `p_paid_on` from a biller is `JV_INVALID_INPUT`; only the owner may send `p_cash_override_reason` (Z1), and a biller who sends one gets `JV_FORBIDDEN`; only the owner may send `original_net_weight` (X8). *Source:* NODE-DESIGN 3.3, 4.3.

- **X0. Lock order and timeouts (every function that writes).**
  1. First statement: the idempotency insert (L5).
  2. The function's own role check (the route wrapper has already checked the session, the must-change-password gate, the matrix row and the body, A6, before the claim of item 1; if this check refuses, the rollback removes the claim row again, L5).
  3. **The customer row `FOR UPDATE`.** Every function that reads or changes a customer's bills, receipts or allocations takes this lock before it reads anything: X1, X2, X3, X4, X5, X10, X11, X12. X4, X5 and X10 find the customer by reading the receipt or the (old) bill first without a lock, then lock the customer, then re-read that row `FOR UPDATE` and check it is unchanged, which means **the same customer**: the customer of a receipt or a bill never changes, so that is the only comparison; every other field (the status, `updated_at`) is read again after the lock and decided on then. If the customer differs the call rolls back and answers `JV_BUSY`. A second void of one receipt, or a void beside a cancel, therefore answers `JV_ALREADY_VOID` or `JV_BILL_CANCELLED`, never a false `JV_BUSY` (a review run saw 7 false `JV_BUSY` in 4,800 attempts when `updated_at` was the comparison and none with the customer comparison). This makes all money work for one customer run one at a time, so two concurrent bills or a bill beside a payment can never both apply the same advance (the review's case A and B). The shared walk-in customer serialises anonymous sales, which is acceptable at shop volume.
  4. Nothing further is locked for the customer's bills, receipts and allocations: the customer lock already lets one writer in at a time (a rebalance cannot lock rows in id order and does not need to).
  5. The pieces (X1, X5, X10, X8, X9), taken by **one statement**, `SELECT ... WHERE id IN (...) ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE`, so that InnoDB locks them in primary-key order [S]. **The order is MariaDB's: ids are never sorted in code, and never one statement for each piece.** The native `UUID` type does not sort like its text: `00000001-0000-4000-8000-000000000000` comes before `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001` in SQL and after it as text [W], so a helper that sorts in code and locks one piece at a time takes its locks in another order than the single statement, and a review run got a deadlock at once with two such connections; the lock-trail check cannot see it, so T-C21 covers it. X10 locks the old bill's pieces and the new lines' pieces in one statement over both lists. X8 and X9 take no customer lock.
  6. **The number counter row last**, after every check (overpayment, cash limit, available pieces) so the counter lock is held for the shortest time and cannot take part in a cycle.
  7. Every connection is set up as S11 says. `innodb_lock_wait_timeout = 5` makes a lock wait longer than 5 seconds fail with error 1205, which the API turns into `ROLLBACK` of the whole transaction and `JV_BUSY` (a 1205 rolls back only the failing statement, S12); the limit applies to every statement of the connection, so it also covers the idempotency insert of item 1. `max_statement_time = 8` cuts one statement (error 1969, also `JV_BUSY`), and the **deadline of 12 seconds** of S12 bounds the whole call, because `max_statement_time` is per statement and does not add up [S]. A deadlock (1213) is re-run by the API up to 2 more times (S12). The screen retries `JV_BUSY` once with the same Idempotency-Key (S8). **These numbers assume the API reaches the database in a millisecond or two.** A call is about 25 round trips (a mean of 22 statements for `create_bill`, 27 for `reissue_bill`, 11 for a payment) and holds the customer lock through most of them; with a delaying proxy in a review run a round trip of 30 ms gave a median call of 1.3 s and no `JV_BUSY`, and 80 ms gave a median of 3.2 s with 17 of 320 contended calls failing as `JV_BUSY`. The Hostinger proof measured the round trip: 0.02 ms for a `SELECT 1` and 0.08 ms for a small transaction, the database being on the same server at `127.0.0.1` [P], so the assumption holds by a wide margin; the pass line of 30 ms stays as the test if the host or the path ever changes (O48). *Test:* T-C10 to T-C13, T-C15, T-C19. *Source:* docs #1, #2, F1, RF-2, RM-6.
  8. **Test hook.** Each money function (X1 to X5, X10 to X12) and the customer archive of A5 (its pause point is `archive_customer.after_check`; the stock functions have no customer state) calls `ctx.pause('<function>.after_read')` once, after it has read the customer's state (only what is open: the bills with something pending and the receipts with something left, by an aggregate query, not the whole history; the walk-in customer collects every anonymous sale for ever, and with 10,000 bills one payment took 120 to 230 ms with the lock held in a review run) and before it allocates, and it allocates from what it read before the hook (a re-read after the hook would hide a missing lock). The hook **exists only in the test build**: the production build does not contain it (T-D29 greps the production bundle), so no request can reach it. For a two-session test the test passes each call a gate it controls (a promise that the test releases): there is **one gate per session**, session 1 is held at the hook (**for less than 3 seconds**, because the lock wait of 5 seconds still cuts session 2) and session 2 is released first, and the test runs in both release orders; with the lock in place session 2 simply waits at the customer lock, so the order of release does not change the result. **Each test names its start state** (T-C09 to T-C14), because a pair can pass without the lock when the final books are the same either way (a void of a part-paid bill beside a payment, an advance voided beside a bill, which a review run showed passing with the lock removed). No database object has to be replaced, committed and restored (draft 3's `create or replace` of `private.test_pause` and its polling of `pg_try_advisory_lock` disappear). A plain wait on the customer row would not prove the lock, because the foreign key of the bill insert may take its own lock on the same row (the documentation fetched says only that foreign-key checks take no gap locks at READ COMMITTED, docs #5). Each of these tests also has a variant in which the customer-lock helper is replaced by a no-op in the test build, and the same test must then fail. *Test:* T-C09 to T-C14, T-C20. *Source:* RM-8, RM3-2; NODE-DESIGN 5.8; RM4-4.
- **X1. `create_bill`** (owner, biller). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_customer_id`, `p_bill_date` (null = today), `p_sale_type`, `p_items` (a list; each: `sku_id`, `metal_rate`, `labour_rate`, `piroi`, `other_charges`, `quantity`; `metal_rate` is required; `labour_rate` is required for a By Weight line and ignored for a Per Piece line; an absent `piroi` or `other_charges` is 0 and an absent `quantity` is 1), `p_discount`, `p_additional`, `p_notes`, `p_payments` (a list; each: `mode`, `amount`, `reference`), `p_confirm_held` (a list of piece ids), `p_cash_override_reason`. Steps after X0 items 1 to 3: (a) validate every input against S4, Y8, R6 and the payment modes (no `Credit`), and that each piece appears **once** in `p_items` (a piece twice is `JV_INVALID_INPUT`, and not the raw duplicate-key error of the `UNIQUE (bill_id, sku_id)` of D12); (b) the customer must exist and not be archived (`JV_CUSTOMER_ARCHIVED`); (c) lock the pieces (one statement, X0 item 5) and refuse any that is not billable (R10) or already on an active bill (`JV_PIECE_UNAVAILABLE`, naming the pieces); (d) price the lines and the bill with R1 to R8 and R14 from the locked piece rows and the given rates, with the bill's pricing version (R13); (e) the explicit payments must not exceed `grand_total` (`JV_OVERPAYMENT`) and the cash rules of section 13 must pass; (f) take the bill number (L3, L4); (g) insert the bill and lines; (h) update the pieces to `Sold` with a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status is billable AND sold_bill_id IS NULL` and check the row count equals the number of lines (defence in depth); (i) insert one `money` receipt and one allocation per payment row, dated the bill date; (j) rebalance the customer (Y6), which applies any advance the customer holds; (k) store the result and return `{bill, items, receipts, allocations, warnings, advance_applied}`, where `warnings` may hold `PAN_MISSING`, `RATE_MISSING`, `RATE_STALE`. *Test:* T-A01 to T-A19, T-B03, T-B07, T-C04 to T-C06, T-C10, T-C11, T-D09. *Source:* hazards 1 and 2, INV-01, BIL-01 to BIL-06, BIL-11, F1, F14.
- **X2. `record_payment`** (owner, biller). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_customer_id`, `p_amount` (greater than 0), `p_mode` (Cash, UPI, Card, Bank Transfer, Other), `p_paid_on`, `p_reference`, `p_notes`, `p_bill_id` (optional), `p_cash_override_reason`. Inserts the receipt, allocates by Y3 (named bill first), keeps any remainder as an advance (for the walk-in customer a payment above its pending dues is refused, K5), applies the cash rules of section 13, rebalances (Y6), and returns `{receipt, allocations, advance_remaining}`. *Test:* T-B04 to T-B06, T-B23, T-C09, T-C10. *Source:* LED-01, LED-04, LED-05, LED-06, F9.
- **X3. `settle_fine`** (owner, biller). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_customer_id`, `p_grams`, `p_mode` (`Silver` or `Cash`), `p_silver_rate` (required for `Cash`), `p_bill_id` (optional), `p_paid_on`, `p_reference`, `p_notes`, `p_cash_override_reason`. For the walk-in customer `p_bill_id` is required and no more grams are settled than that bill's fine pending (K5, O52). Checks SF2, creates the `fine_silver` or `fine_cash` receipt (a `fine_cash` receipt carries `amount = round(grams × rate, 2)` and mode `Cash`), allocates grams by Y3, applies the cash rules to `fine_cash`, rebalances, and returns the receipt, allocations and the remaining fine. *Test:* T-B11 to T-B13, T-B17, T-B23. *Source:* LED-02, LED-11, LED-12; RM4-16.
- **X4. `void_receipt`** (owner). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_receipt_id`, `p_reason` (at least 3 characters). The receipt must be `active` (`JV_ALREADY_VOID`). Marks it voided (with `voided_at`, `voided_on` and the person, D13); its allocations stop counting; a voided refund restores the advance (Y10); then rebalances the customer (Y6), so money that was waiting as an advance now pays a bill that the void re-opened. It raises `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` ("void the refund first") when the customer's advance after the void would be below 0, because the money was already handed back; a payment that bounced after a refund is booked as dues with X12. Returns the receipt, the allocations made by the rebalance and the affected bills' derived status. *Test:* T-B08, T-B09, T-B16, T-B20, T-C12, T-D09. *Source:* LED-02.
- **X5. `cancel_bill`** (owner). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_bill_id`, `p_reason` (at least 3 characters). The bill must be `active` (`JV_BILL_CANCELLED`); an `opening_balance` bill may be cancelled (IM5). For the walk-in customer a bill that has live allocations is refused with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` (K5: void its receipts first). Applies K3, then rebalances (Y6). Returns the bill, the pieces returned to stock and the allocations released. *Test:* T-B10, T-C13, T-D09. *Source:* BIL-12.
- **X6. `create_customer`** (owner, biller). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, name, mobile (required), customer type, email, address, GST number, PAN, notes. Creates the customer (never the walk-in). Returns the customer. Opening balances are X12. *Test:* T-D09, T-F06. *Source:* hazard 1 (a new customer is saved before the bill that names them), AC5.
- **X7. `quote_bill`** (owner, biller; read-only, no `Idempotency-Key`, so it takes no locks and writes nothing; it reads the piece rows and the rates with plain `SELECT`s). Inputs: `p_bill_date`, `p_sale_type`, `p_items`, `p_discount`, `p_additional`, `p_pricing_version` (default: the current one). Returns the lines, the totals, the list prices and the warnings that `create_bill` would produce, using the same `priceBill` of `pricing.ts`. Used by tests and as a check against the screen's preview (C12). *Test:* T-A15.
- **X8. `create_design`** (owner, staff). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_design_id` (made by the screen, P2; refused if it exists), `p_design` (category, subcategory, name, material, purity, selling method, description, notes, tags, photo ids; checked as P2 says), `p_pieces` (groups, each with `pieces` 1 to 100, weights, stones, kundan, piroi, three labour rates, notes; `original_net_weight` only if the caller is the owner, otherwise `JV_FORBIDDEN`). Creates the design (number from `design:<prefix>`), each piece (number from `sku:<design_no>`, barcode `<prefix>-<sku_no>`, status `In Stock`, location the first entry of `settings.locations`, which is the constant `BUSINESS_LOCATION` as coded, `AddDesignClient.tsx:1129`, AC3), and the owner-only weight rows; applies R1's blank-rate rule; skips numbers that already exist (L3). At most 500 pieces per call. Returns the design and pieces. *Test:* T-C03, T-C17, T-D03, T-D21, T-F01. *Source:* INV-09, INV-10, hazard 4, F16.
- **X9. `set_sku_status`** (owner, staff). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_sku_ids` (a list of piece ids), `p_status`, `p_note`. May set any status except `Sold`; a piece that is `Sold` cannot be changed (use X5 or X10). Setting `Archived` also sets `archived_at`; leaving `Archived` clears it (D8). Returns the updated pieces. *Test:* T-C07, T-C08, T-C17. *Source:* INV-01, `EditStockClient.tsx:152-154`, F7.
- **X10. `reissue_bill`** (owner). The **correction path**: cancel a wrong bill and save the right one in one transaction, keeping the money. Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_old_bill_id`, `p_reason`, then the inputs of X1 except `p_customer_id` (the customer is the old bill's; it cannot change). The old bill must be `active` and `estimate` (a bill that is already cancelled, including one already re-issued, raises `JV_BILL_CANCELLED`, K6). Steps after X0: lock the pieces of the old bill and of the new lines together, in one statement over both lists (X0 item 5); cancel the old bill (K3, without its own rebalance); create the new bill with `reissue_of = p_old_bill_id` and the rules of X1; then rebalance with **the new bill first**: the customer's unallocated money and silver grams, including what the cancelled bill had received, go to the new bill before any other bill (Y3 with a named bill), oldest receipt first; then the explicit new payments (X1(i)) are recorded, and the overpayment check counts what was carried over. For the walk-in customer a surplus of carried-over **money over the new bill's total, or of silver grams over its fine due,** is refused (K5: void the receipts first). Returns both bills and the allocations. *Test:* T-B17, T-B19, T-C13, T-C21, T-D09. *Source:* F2, K1; RM4-1.
- **X11. `issue_refund`** (owner). **The first thing to leave M1 if the calendar slips.** Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_customer_id`, `p_amount`, `p_mode`, `p_paid_on`, `p_reference`, `p_notes`. Under the customer lock, checks Y10 (amount not more than the customer's advance), inserts the `refund` receipt, rebalances (nothing to apply), and returns the receipt and the new advance. *Test:* T-B20, T-D09. *Source:* F2.
- **X12. `add_opening_balance`** (owner). Inputs: the `Idempotency-Key` header, `p_customer_id`, `p_dues`, `p_fine_due`, `p_advance`, `p_fine_advance` (all at least 0, not all 0), `p_as_of`. Under the customer lock creates the `opening_balance` bill when dues or fine due are greater than 0 (number `OB-nnnn`, D15) and an `is_opening` receipt for each advance (money with mode `Other`, or `fine_silver`), then rebalances (an advance and dues of the same customer net off). `p_advance` and `p_fine_advance` are refused for the walk-in (K5). Used for corrections after the import (IM5) and for customers created later; the import itself runs the same `rebalance` code (IM4). *Test:* T-B21, T-D09. *Source:* F4.
- **X13.** *(retired in draft 4)* There is no advisor and no `anon` or `authenticated` role to warn about. The same expectation is the function matrix of T-D09 (who may call which function) and the static checks of A7. *Test:* T-D11.

## 11. Derived views

The four derived reads, V1 to V4, are **queries in the API's read layer, not database views**: with Delete and Drop unticked (S7) a view can be created once and never replaced, altered or dropped, because those statements need the Drop right (tested, error 1142 [W]), so a view would be frozen at its first definition and a later fix would need Harsh to tick Drop; the migration rule of S9 therefore has no view in it. The names V1 to V4 and the column names below are kept from draft 3, so that the screens and the tests keep their words, and the nightly check I4 calls the same query functions. The API reads them with the caller's field allow-list and refuses them to staff and to callers with no session (A4); the owner and the biller can read them. Nothing in them is stored (S3). Each is read in pages of 1,000 in a total order, so paging is stable (C3). V1 and V2 expose the base row's `updated_at`, which the touches move whenever a payment or an allocation changes (D23, C4). A window-function query (V3) is plain SQL and needs no special right [S].

Two words used below. A **live allocation** is a `receipt_allocations` row whose `released_at` is null and whose receipt is `active`. Money allocations come from `money` receipts and carry `amount`; fine allocations come from `fine_silver` and `fine_cash` receipts and carry `fine_grams` (their `amount` is 0). A `refund` receipt never has allocations.

- **V1. `bill_summary`**: one row per bill: `id`, `bill_no`, `customer_id`, `bill_date`, `doc_type`, `sale_type`, `status`, `reissue_of`, `grand_total`, `received` (the sum of live money allocations to the bill), `pending`, `fine_due` (`fine_weight_due`), `fine_settled` (the sum of live fine allocations), `fine_pending`, `payment_status` (R11), `item_count`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. For an active bill `pending = grand_total - received` and `fine_pending = fine_due - fine_settled`; for a cancelled bill `received`, `pending`, `fine_settled` and `fine_pending` are all 0 (K3 released its allocations). *Test:* T-B02, T-B10, T-B15.
- **V2. `customer_balances`**: one row per customer, the walk-in customer included, keyed by `id` (the customer's id, which the cache merges by). Rupees: `billed` (sum of `grand_total` of active bills, opening bills included), `total_purchases` (the same without `opening_balance` bills), `received` (sum of `amount` of active `money` receipts, opening advances included), `refunded` (sum of active `refund` receipts), `allocated` (sum of live money allocations), then `outstanding = billed - allocated`, `advance = received - allocated - refunded`, and `balance = billed - received + refunded`. Always `balance = outstanding - advance`; after Y6 at most one of `outstanding` and `advance` is above zero (I10), and then `outstanding = max(0, balance)` and `advance = max(0, -balance)`. Silver: `fine_billed` (sum of `fine_weight_due` of active bills), `fine_received` (sum of `fine_grams` of active fine receipts), `fine_allocated`, `fine_outstanding = fine_billed - fine_allocated`, `fine_advance = fine_received - fine_allocated`, `fine_balance = fine_billed - fine_received`. Also `last_purchase` (the latest `bill_date` of an active, non-opening bill) and the customer row's `updated_at`. `outstanding` and `advance` come from allocations while `balance` comes from totals; the two routes must agree (I4, I10). **A read of one customer** (the customer card, the state read of X0 item 8) is written with correlated sub-selects over `bills`, `receipts` and `receipt_allocations` filtered by that customer's id and backed by `receipt_allocations (customer_id)` (D19); the derived-table form that totals everyone at once is used only for the full hydrate, because in a review run it read three whole tables for one customer (22 to 24 ms at 5,000 bills against 0.9 ms by sub-selects). *Test:* T-B05, T-B15, T-B17, T-B20, T-B21.
- **V3. `customer_ledger`**: one row per event of a customer, with a running `balance` and `fine_balance` (debit minus credit, summed in event order, `partition by customer_id`, so a filter on one customer is applied before the sums). Events: `Invoice` for each bill, cancelled ones too (`Opening` for an `opening_balance` bill): dated `bill_date`, debit `grand_total`, fine debit `fine_weight_due`. `Cancellation` for each cancelled bill: dated `cancelled_on` (D11), credit `grand_total`, fine credit `fine_weight_due`. `Payment` for each `money` receipt (`Opening advance` when `is_opening`): dated `paid_on`, credit `amount`. `Fine` for each `fine_silver` or `fine_cash` receipt: dated `paid_on`, fine credit `fine_grams`, no rupee entry (for `fine_cash` the description says "settled in cash at rate X = amount Y"). `Refund` for each `refund` receipt: dated `paid_on`, debit `amount`. `Void` for each voided receipt: dated `voided_on` (D13), the exact reverse of its own row. **The order key** is (`entry_date`, `entry_at`, `kind_rank`, `id`): `entry_at` is the row's `created_at` (`cancelled_at` or `voided_at` for the last two kinds); `created_at` of bills and receipts is set from the API's clock, which never returns the same instant twice (S5), so rows made in one transaction already differ; `kind_rank` makes the order total when two rows still tie (Invoice 1, Cancellation 2, Payment and Fine 3, Refund 4, Void 5), and `id` is the last tie-break. After the last row, `balance` equals V2's `balance` and `fine_balance` equals V2's `fine_balance` (I4). *Test:* T-B14, T-B16, T-B25. *Source:* LED-04, LED-10, F18; RM4-9.
- **V4. `daily_totals`**: one row per (`business_date`, `mode`) for the modes Cash, UPI, Card, Bank Transfer and Other, with `money_in`, `money_out` and `net = money_in - money_out`. A `money` or `fine_cash` receipt adds `money_in` on its `paid_on` (so the Cash row includes silver settled in cash, Y1). A `refund` adds `money_out` on its `paid_on`. A voided receipt adds the **reverse** amount on its `voided_on` (D13) (a voided payment adds `money_out`, a voided refund adds `money_in`; an `is_opening` receipt, which V4 never counted, adds nothing), so a void never changes a day already closed (a back-dated receipt or refund by the owner, Y8, does: O3). Opening advances (`is_opening`) are not money received today and are left out (D15). The owner's evening check is three figures: today's Cash `net`, today's UPI `net`, and the sum of V2 `outstanding`. *Test:* T-B15, T-B16, T-B20, T-B21. *Source:* reviewer risk 1, F4.
- **V5. One set of selectors.** The dashboard, the reports and the customer card compute their figures from V1 to V4 through a single browser module, `src/lib/selectors.ts` (C14). **Sales** for a business date or month are the sum of `grand_total` of V1 rows with `status = 'active'` and `doc_type <> 'opening_balance'`, grouped by `bill_date`; cancelled bills are shown on their own line (count and value; a cancelled `opening_balance` bill is not counted there, because an opening-balance bill is never a sale, C14) and are not sales; a cancelled bill therefore leaves the day it was billed on (sales are a report, and only the cash book of V4 is kept stable for closed days). The module has a unit test over a fixed data set that checks every figure. *Test:* T-B15. *Source:* LED-10, DAT-08.

## 12. Invariants and the nightly check

`runInvariants()` (SQL statements run by the API; I6 and I11 are completed in Node because they re-price with `pricing.ts`) returns the rows that break each rule as (`invariant`, `row_ref`, `detail`); an empty result is a pass. It only reads. It has one snapshot of the data for its whole run: the job sets `REPEATABLE READ` for its own session and then starts `START TRANSACTION WITH CONSISTENT SNAPSHOT` (docs #5; at READ COMMITTED every statement would read its own snapshot [S]), so a payment saved while it runs cannot cause a false alarm.

- **I1.** Every `Sold` piece has a `sold_bill_id` of an `active` bill that has a line for it, and every line of an active bill points to a `Sold` piece with that bill id. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18.
- **I2.** No piece is on the lines of two active bills. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18.
- **I3.** For every active bill, live money allocations never exceed `grand_total` and live fine allocations never exceed `fine_weight_due`; for every active receipt, its live allocations never exceed its `amount` (money) or its `fine_grams` (fine); no allocation that is not released points to a cancelled bill; a fine allocation has `amount` 0 and a money allocation has `fine_grams` 0. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18.
- **I4.** For every customer, `balance` and `fine_balance` of V2 equal an independent recomputation from the base tables (`billed - received + refunded`, and the same for silver) and equal the last running balances in V3. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18.
- **I5.** Bill numbers are unique and, within each series (prefix and year, and `OB-`), the numbers issued by a function (those with no `import_batch_id`) are contiguous from the series' `seed_value + 1` (`seed_value` is 0 for a new series and is set by `seedCounters()` after an import, IM3), so an `OB-` number made by X12 after the import does not break the check; `seedCounters()` only raises a `seed_value`, and numbers at or below it are ignored. *Test:* T-B18, T-C16. *Source:* RF-6, RF3-5.
- **I6.** For every bill that has lines (opening bills have none, IM4): each stored line amount (`metal_amount`, `labour_amount`, `fine_weight_due`, `item_total`) equals a fresh run of `priceLine` of `pricing.ts` for the bill's `pricing_version` over the stored snapshot (R13); `subtotal` equals the sum of the lines and `fine_weight_due` the sum of the lines' fine due; `grand_total` equals `round(subtotal - discount + additional_charges, 0)`; and `round_off` is either 0 or `grand_total - (subtotal - discount + additional_charges)`, whichever the setting at the time of saving gave (so changing the setting later cannot fail the check). *Test:* T-B18, T-A15, T-C16.
- **I7.** No negative amount, weight or quantity exists in any table (except `round_off`, D11), no receipt has the mode `Credit`, no photo value starts with `data:`, and every `kind`, `mode` and `status` combination is one the checks of D13 allow (a guard in case a constraint is lost in a later migration). *Test:* T-B18.
- **I8. Nightly job.** One **hourly tick** drives all scheduled work. An hPanel cron job of the **Custom** type calls it every hour with `curl -fsS -X POST -H "Authorization: Bearer <token>" https://<api name>/internal/jobs/tick` (the cron runs in UTC, at the shortest once a minute, and fired 1 to 2 seconds after the minute [P]; the command, token included, is visible in hPanel to whoever can open it and is worth only the tick), and a scheduled GitHub Actions workflow calls it independently (it does nothing if the first call worked; GitHub's scheduled runs can be late or skipped and Hostinger may filter outside callers [U], so O48 tests both). The Hostinger **Node.js cron type is not used**: it ran the app's Node 24 with the app's environment, but a job of that type did not appear in hPanel's list and could not be deleted [P]. The endpoint needs a bearer token (the environment holds only its hash), is throttled (A13), sends no CORS headers and refuses an `Origin` header. **One run at a time:** the tick first takes `GET_LOCK('jv_tick', 0)` on one connection of the pool of S11, which it keeps for the whole tick (it opens no connection of its own), and answers "already running" at once if the lock is taken (a MariaDB lock ends with its connection, so a crash or a redeploy never leaves it stuck; `GET_LOCK` is allowed to the plan's user [W] and untried on Hostinger [U]). **Phases:** `nightly_check`, `db_dump`, `photo_copy` and `housekeeping` (L6), each with one row in `sys_job_runs` for the business date in Asia/Kolkata (unique on `job` and `run_date`), so a retry is an update and not a new row: `failed` goes back to `running` on the next tick, a `running` row whose `started_at` is older than 10 minutes counts as abandoned and is taken over, and `ok` ends the phase for that date. A phase is due when it is not `ok` for today's date and, for the nightly work, it is after 02:00 in Asia/Kolkata (computed in the API); so no time-zone assumption about cron is made, a late or missed hour costs nothing, and a second call the same day answers "already done". **Slices:** a tick has about 50 seconds (its own handler timer, A14; Hostinger's front end cuts a request at about 60 seconds whatever the app does [P]) and its own statement limit of 20 seconds (S11); each phase works in slices of about 40 seconds, records where it stopped in `detail`, and the next tick goes on; the `backup_runs` heartbeat says `ok` only for a complete phase (B2, D17). The work runs inside the request and not as a background task, because a background task would die silently if the process were stopped. The nightly check runs `runInvariants()` and writes a `check_runs` row (`ok`, and `details` with the count per invariant and at most 20 sample rows each). MariaDB's event scheduler cannot do this job: it is off on the plan, a normal user cannot switch it on, and an event created while it is off is accepted but never runs [H][S]. **Alarm for a missed tick:** the owner's dashboard line turns red after 26 hours, but only when she opens it, which is not an alarm. So the GitHub Actions workflow also calls the read-only `GET /internal/jobs/health` (same token), which answers `503` when the last good export is older than 26 hours, and the workflow then fails; GitHub e-mails the repository owner about a failed run at no cost [N]. A second outside monitor (an uptime service calling the same endpoint) is O44's open choice. Otherwise nobody is paged in M1: Harsh reads the check each morning for the first two weeks (plan task P4-T1). *Test:* T-B18, T-R04. *Source:* reviewer risk 1; PART-A-MARIADB-MAPPING.md section 2; NODE-DESIGN 7.3 (second round); RF4-3, RF4-6, RF4-15.
- **I9.** The same function runs inside the test suite after random sequences of operations (T-B14) and after deliberate damage (T-B18), so a rule that drifts fails in tests before it can fail at the counter. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18.
- **I10. The rebalance post-condition (Y6).** For every customer: the sum of V1 `pending` over their active bills equals V2 `outstanding`, and the sum of V1 `fine_pending` equals `fine_outstanding`; `outstanding`, `advance`, `fine_outstanding` and `fine_advance` are not negative; if `advance` is above zero then `outstanding` is 0, and if `fine_advance` is above zero then `fine_outstanding` is 0; and the walk-in customer's `advance` and `fine_advance` are 0 (K5). *Test:* T-B14, T-B17, T-B18, T-B26, T-C10, T-C11. *Source:* F1 (case B).
- **I11. The review line (a report, not an invariant; it never turns the check red).** For the previous business day: the bill lines sold below the server's list prices (R14), the bills with a discount above the constant `discount_flag_percent` (10) percent of the subtotal, the bills and receipts saved with a cash override reason (Z1), the cancellations, re-issues, voids and refunds, the walk-in's cash total and number of bills of the day (Z2 does not add them up, so this is where a split sale shows), the `fine_cash` settlements whose rate is more than the constant `rate_flag_percent` (5) percent away from every Silver rate of that day (the latest rate dated not after it, per purity; no Silver rate at all counts as away), the daily rates entered by a biller that moved more than that percent from the previous rate of the same material and purity, and the bills whose sale type differs from the customer's type (R9). The counts go into `check_runs.details`; the owner can open the lists (up to 50 rows each). Nothing is blocked (O26). *Test:* T-B24. *Source:* F12.
- **I12. Backup heartbeat (B2) and the download flag (B3).** The dashboard line shows how many hours ago the last `backup_runs` row with `ok` for `db_dump`, and for `photo_copy`, was written, the size of the backup folder, the database size against the 2 GB red line (worked out as P5 says and not from `information_schema` alone), and **how many days ago the owner last used Download everything** (B3): counted from `settings.last_download`, or, when there is none, from the oldest `check_runs` row (the first night the system ran). That figure is **amber from 28 days** (the reminder) and **red after 35 days** (O53). The health query `readHealth` works out the figure and the reasons; the route only repeats them. *Test:* T-R03, T-R05. *Source:* F15; Harsh, 15 Oct 2026.
- **I13. Cash book tie-out.** For each mode, the sum of V4 `net` over all dates equals the sum of the active `money` and `fine_cash` receipts of that mode that are not `is_opening`, minus the active refunds of that mode. It guards the void-day logic of V4. *Test:* T-B14, T-B18. *Source:* RM-13.

**The dashboard line (owner only)** shows, in one place: "Books: OK at 02:00" or "Problem" (I8), the age of the last good dump and photo copy (I12), the database size and the size of the backup folder (I12), the days since the last Download everything (I12, B3), and yesterday's review counts (I11). It turns **red** when the last check says Problem, when the last `check_runs` row is older than 26 hours (the job stopped), when the last good dump or photo copy is older than 26 hours, when the database is above 2 GB, or when the last Download everything is older than 35 days; it turns amber when the database user has a Delete or Drop right on (S11) or when the last Download everything is older than 28 days. *Test:* T-B18, T-R03, T-R05.

**Mutation tests (T-B18).** For each of I1 to I7, I10 and I13 a test damages exactly one row with a direct SQL statement on the local test database and expects `runInvariants()` to name that invariant and that row; with no damage it returns nothing. Damage by data is undone by resetting the test database; damage that needs a dropped `CHECK` runs on a schema built for that one test and thrown away afterwards, because MariaDB DDL commits at once and cannot be rolled back (docs #3; draft 3 dropped the constraint inside a rolled-back transaction). I13 reads the same rows as V4, so no single damaged row can fire it: its test passes `runInvariants()` an injected, old and wrong version of the V4 query (a void of an opening receipt reversing money V4 never counted) and expects I13 to fire; the queries of section 11 are code, so no view has to be replaced.

## 13. Cash limit and PAN prompt

Not legal or tax advice. The figures, the comparison (at or above, or above) and the legal posture are for Siddhi's CA `[CA]`; the reading used here comes from secondary sources only (research row 19, open item (j) of section 0). The thresholds are constants in the API's constants file `limits` (`cash_limit = 200000`, `pan_threshold = 200000`, `discount_flag_percent = 10`, `rate_flag_percent = 5`) so a decision changes one number in one file.

- **Z1. Cash from a named customer.** For a customer that is not the walk-in, let `cash_today` be the sum of `amount` of their active receipts with `paid_on` on one business date that are `money` receipts of mode `Cash` or `fine_cash` receipts, leaving out `is_opening` rows and not reduced by refunds. When a function (X1 for the cash payment rows of a new bill, X10 through the rules of X1, X2 for a `Cash` payment, X3 for `fine_cash`) would make `cash_today` **reach `cash_limit` or more**, it raises `JV_CASH_LIMIT`, unless the caller is the **owner** and passes `p_cash_override_reason` of at least 10 characters. A biller cannot override: the owner enters that payment. The reason is saved on the bill (`cash_override_reason`) or on the receipt (`cash_override_reason`) and audited (L1, I11). Only the per-day total is built. Not built: the limb for one transaction paid in several parts, possibly over several days (which a per-day sum does not see), and the limb for one event or occasion (O14). *Test:* T-A12, T-B23. *Source:* CMP-08, F9.
- **Z2. The walk-in customer.** The walk-in row stands for many different people, so its cash is never added up over a day. Instead: a single cash payment (one `record_payment`, one `fine_cash` settlement, or the cash rows of one bill) of `cash_limit` or more for the walk-in raises `JV_CASH_LIMIT` with the message "Select or create the customer"; and X1 refuses a bill whose `grand_total` is `pan_threshold` or more for the walk-in with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` and the same message (the PAN rule says "exceeding"; the stricter reading is deliberate and O14 asks the CA). There is no override for the walk-in: the owner creates the customer first. The walk-in never holds a PAN (D10). *Test:* T-A12, T-B23. *Source:* K5, F9.
- **Z3. PAN prompt.** When a bill's `grand_total` is `pan_threshold` or more and the named customer has no `pan`, X1 still saves the bill and returns the warning `PAN_MISSING`; the screen asks for the PAN and saves it on the customer (a content column, A5). A PAN is ten characters: five capital letters, four digits, one capital letter; anything else is refused by the `CHECK` on the customer row (D10), and the screen validates before it sends. It is a prompt, not a block, because a buyer may legitimately have no PAN and give a declaration instead. *Test:* T-A12. *Source:* CMP-08.
- **Z4.** Neither rule creates a new printable document (Harsh's ruling on "documents"); a buyer's declaration, if one is needed, is kept on paper outside the system `[CA]`. *Test:* none. *Source:* Harsh's ruling of 5 October.

## 14. QA findings to requirements

Words used in the "Handling" column. **Closed**: a requirement and a test remove the cause. **Partly closed**: some of the cause is removed and the rest is named. **Kept as coded**: the rule is deliberately unchanged and flagged. **M1 elsewhere**: another M1 plan task removes it (named). **M2**: the cause leaves with a feature that is not in M1. **Open in M1**: the finding stays open in M1 and the broken control is hidden. **Not decided `[CA]`**: only Siddhi's CA can decide.

## 15. Test list

Types: **DB** a test against a real MariaDB (the local Docker container, version 11.8, data in tmpfs, started fresh for the suite) that calls the service function with the caller's role in its context; each test starts from the migrated empty schema and resets its tables, because a service function commits its own transaction and MariaDB DDL cannot be rolled back (draft 3's tests rolled each test back and impersonated a database role); **DB2** a database test with two or more real connections (needed for locks), which resets its own data afterwards; **HTTP** a route test through the API's in-process request injection, with one login per role (owner, biller, staff, a deactivated user and no session); **UNIT** a Vitest test; **E2E** a Playwright test (two browsers where noted); **BUILD** a script over the build output, the migrations, the route table or the source; **DRILL** a checklist run by hand and logged. "Fail first" means the test is written and seen failing before the code exists (SDD, implement step). The database tests run on the local container; what they cannot prove about Hostinger itself is the job of the Hostinger proof (O48).

### A. Pricing (19)
| ID | Type | What it proves | Req |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-A01 | DB | The worked example: 35.42 g × 6,920, labour 353/g, piroi 2.15, Retail, By Weight gives metal 245,106.40, labour 12,503.26, line 257,611.81, grand total 257,612 | R1 to R8 |
| T-A02 | DB + UNIT | The labour rate chains: in `create_design` a blank semi-wholesale rate takes wholesale then retail and a blank wholesale rate takes retail (DB); on the screen the default labour rate for a sale type falls back as coded, Wholesale semi then retail then the old single labour, Semi-Wholesale semi, wholesale, retail, labour (UNIT) | R1 |
| T-A03 | DB + UNIT | The bill stores the sale type it was sent and prices each line with the labour rate it was sent (DB); on the screen, choosing a customer sets the bill's sale type and the default labour rate follows it (UNIT) | R1, R9 |
| T-A04 | DB | Wholesale silver By Weight: NW 13.9 g, labour 40/g gives metal 0, labour 556.00, fine due 13.900, grand total 556, with piroi and other charges 0; the same for a 925-purity piece (purity changes nothing); Semi-Wholesale silver and Wholesale gold give fine 0 | R4, R5 |
| T-A05 | DB | Wholesale silver Per Piece: the price is kept and fine is 0 (QA BIL-06): 2 pieces at 1,200 give metal 2,400.00, fine 0 | R4 |
| T-A06 | DB | Per Piece, quantity 3, price 1,200: metal 3,600.00, labour 0, quantity saved on the line (BIL-05); By Weight with quantity 2 is rejected | R5, R6 |
| T-A07 | DB | Piroi and other charges are added once per line, not per quantity | R7 |
| T-A08 | DB | `before_round` below 0 is rejected; a discount equal to subtotal plus additional gives grand total 0 | R8 |
| T-A09 | DB | Every negative or over-cap input is rejected with `JV_INVALID_INPUT`: weights, rates, piroi, charges, discount, additional, quantity, payment, refund, opening-balance and `fine_cash` amounts; more than 2 decimals on a rate or an amount (a trailing zero counts: `1.230` is refused) and more than 3 on a weight (`1.230` is accepted, `1.2300` is refused); a By Weight line without `labour_rate` is refused, and an absent `piroi`, `other_charges` and `quantity` mean 0, 0 and 1; a JSON number (not a string) in a money or weight field is refused (S3), and so are the texts `.5`, `5.`, ` 7.50 ` (with spaces) and `+5`, by the Zod layer before `pricing.ts` runs (a direct call of `parseRupees` still accepts them, which is why the layer exists); the same piece twice in one bill is `JV_INVALID_INPUT` and not a raw duplicate-key error (X1 (a)) | S4, S3, X1 |
| T-A10 | DB | Round-off on and off both give a whole-rupee grand total; 1,000.50 gives 1,001 and 1,000.49 gives 1,000; `round_off` is stored negative for 1,000.40 (−0.40) and is 0 when the setting is off | R8, D11 |
| T-A11 | DB | Paise are exact: 4.6 g × 92.50 = 425.50, grand total 426; random decimal sums show no drift | S3 |
| T-A12 | DB | In `create_bill`: cash of 1,99,999.99 on a day passes and 2,00,000 raises `JV_CASH_LIMIT` for a biller; the owner with a 10-character reason saves it and the reason is stored; a bill of 2,00,000 or more for a customer with no PAN returns `PAN_MISSING`; a walk-in bill of 2,00,000 or more is refused with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` | Z1, Z2, Z3 |
| T-A13 | DB | The server's list price follows material and purity (an 18K and a 22K piece get their own rates) and the latest rate dated not after the bill's date; a backdated bill gets the rate of that date | D4, R2, R14 |
| T-A14 | UNIT + BUILD | The API's `pricing.ts` is byte-identical to the app's (SHA-256 check, `check:pricing`); the app's pricing unit tests also pass against the API's copy; `GET /v1/auth/me` returns `pricing_sha256` and the screen disables Save on bills when it differs from its own; and 200 random inputs priced by it equal an independent exact-decimal oracle (the BigInt oracle and the SQL `DECIMAL` version of the spike) | R12, C12 |
| T-A15 | DB | `quote_bill` returns exactly what `create_bill` then stores, for version 1 and for a test-only version 2 registered for that test only through an injected version table (so the dispatch by version can fail); `quote_bill` writes nothing and takes no row lock (checked in the statement log) | X7, R13 |
| T-A16 | DB | A 40-line bill totals the sum of its lines; 200 lines are accepted, 201 rejected | S4 |
| T-A17 | DB | `metal_rate` is required; a rate that is missing returns the warning `RATE_MISSING` and one dated before the bill's date returns `RATE_STALE`; both still save | D4, R2 |
| T-A18 | DB | Computed amounts are capped: 100,000 g at a rate of 100,000,000 gives `JV_INVALID_INPUT`, not an overflow; a line total, and the subtotal, of exactly 1,000,000,000.00 is accepted and one paisa more is rejected; for the **grand total**, a subtotal of 1,000,000,000.00 plus additional charges of 0.49 is accepted (grand total 1,000,000,000.00, round-off -0.49) and 0.50 is refused | S4 |
| T-A19 | DB | Per-line paise rounding: twenty hand-calculated lines, some of which change the grand total by ₹1 against an unrounded sum; every stored line amount has two decimals | R7 |
