### C. Numbers, life cycle, concurrency (21)
| ID | Type | What it proves | Req |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-C01 | DB + DB2 | 50 sequential and 10 concurrent bills give unique, contiguous numbers; a failed save uses no number; a brand-new series whose first user rolls back while two others wait gives no deadlock; the series row is made by a read first, so a bill is not held up behind the bill before it (with the counter row held for 6.5 seconds the second call answers `JV_BUSY` after 5 seconds through the error map of S8 and not a raw 1205); the number is the `insertId` of the counter `UPDATE` and stays right after an audit row has been inserted (a `SELECT LAST_INSERT_ID()` would not); a counter `UPDATE` that changes no row answers `JV_INTERNAL`; the function result carries every number and id | L3, S2, C11 |
| T-C02 | DB + HTTP | No role can delete a customer; archiving is refused while outstanding, fine outstanding, advance or fine advance is not zero | K4, S7 |
| T-C03 | DB | Design and piece numbers are never reused after archiving; a design id from the screen that already exists is refused | L4, P2, X8 |
| T-C04 | DB2 | Two concurrent `create_bill` on the same piece: exactly one succeeds, the other gets `JV_PIECE_UNAVAILABLE`, no number is used | K2, X1, D8 |
| T-C05 | DB | The same `Idempotency-Key` twice creates one bill and returns the same result (with `Idempotent-Replay: true`); another caller, or the same key with a different cart, is refused; the nightly job empties the result of a request row older than 90 days and a repeat of that key is then refused | L5, L6 |
| T-C06 | DB | A failed `create_bill` (for example an overpayment) leaves no piece Sold and no number used | K2, C5 |
| T-C07 | DB + HTTP | A stock edit (through `PATCH`) cannot change `status` or `archived_at` of a piece; an edit with a stale `updated_at` changes nothing | A5, X9 |
| T-C08 | DB | Repair, Missing, Damaged, Archived, Transferred, Returned and Sold pieces cannot be billed; Reserved and On Approval only with the confirm list; leaving Archived clears `archived_at` | D8, R10, X9 |
| T-C09 | DB2 | Using the test hook (one gate per session, both release orders): start state: one unpaid bill of 1,000; two concurrent payments of 600 for that customer allocate without applying the same pending amount twice, and with the customer lock removed the same test fails | X2 |
| T-C10 | DB2 | Using the test hook (both release orders), start state: an advance of 300; `create_bill` of 400 beside `record_payment` of 500 for the same customer: the result satisfies I3 and I10 and the sum of V1 pending equals V2 outstanding, and with the customer lock removed the same test fails | X0, I10 |
| T-C11 | DB2 | Using the test hook (both release orders), start state: an advance of 300; `create_bill` of 400 beside `issue_refund` of 300 for one customer who holds that advance (a writer that takes no number, because the bill-number counter would serialise two `create_bill` calls and hide a missing customer lock): the advance is either spent on the bill or refunded, never both, so no refund exceeds the advance and I10 holds; with the customer lock removed the same test fails | X0, X1, X11 |
| T-C12 | DB2 | Using the test hook (both release orders), start states: (a) a bill of 1,000 paid fully by receipt R1, `void_receipt` of R1 beside `record_payment` of 500; (b) the same plus a spare advance of 300, `void_receipt` of R1 beside `create_bill` of 400 (the start of a part-paid bill voided beside a payment, and the start of an advance voided beside a bill, pass with the lock removed and are not used); (c) two `void_receipt` calls of one receipt at once, with different keys, give one success and one `JV_ALREADY_VOID` and never `JV_BUSY` (X0 item 3): no deadlock, the invariants hold, and with the customer lock removed the same test fails | X0, X4 |
| T-C13 | DB2 | Using the test hook (both release orders), start states: (a) a bill of 1,000 paid fully, `cancel_bill` beside `create_bill` of 400; (b) a bill of 1,000 with a receipt of 600, `cancel_bill` beside `record_payment` of 400; (c) the same start as (b), `reissue_bill` beside `record_payment` of 400: no deadlock, the invariants hold, and with the customer lock removed the same test fails | X0, X5, X10 |
| T-C14 | DB2 | Using the test hook, two identical requests in flight at the same moment, the first held at its hook for less than 3 seconds: exactly one executes and the second returns the stored result, not an error; with the first held for 6.5 seconds the second answers `JV_BUSY` after the 5-second lock wait and its retry with the same key then returns the stored result | L5 |
| T-C15 | DB2 + HTTP | A lock held for more than 5 seconds by another connection raises `JV_BUSY` (503 with `Retry-After`) and nothing is changed; a statement running past `max_statement_time` (error 1969) does the same; the retry with the same Idempotency-Key succeeds afterwards; a stress run of 8 connections and 200 mixed calls (bills, payments, fine, voids, cancels, re-issues, refunds) over 3 customers, the walk-in among them, and 12 pieces ends with no deadlock (1213) and I1 to I10 true (the spike ran 8 workers of 150 operations in four seeds with 0 deadlocks [S]) | S8, X0 |
| T-C16 | DB | Import: explicit numbers are stored as given; numbering skips an imported number by looking ahead with a read, so no duplicate-key error is raised and no next-key lock is left behind (a second connection inserting into the gap does not wait), and a duplicate-key error on a bill's number answers `JV_INTERNAL` (L3); counter seeding makes new numbers continue; a repeated batch name is refused; I5 and I6 leave imported bills alone; the reconciliation counts equal the source; a code typed in either case finds the piece; a missing semi-wholesale rate becomes the average; after the import an X12 call and a `create_bill` keep I5 green, and I10 is true for a customer who was given both dues and an advance | IM1 to IM4, IM6, D21, L3, R1 |
| T-C17 | DB | `create_design`: 500 pieces are accepted and 501 refused; the original weight from staff is refused with `JV_FORBIDDEN`; `set_sku_status` cannot change a Sold piece and sets `archived_at` together with Archived | X8, X9 |
| T-C18 | DB2 | *New in draft 4.* Restart safety: a process stopped with `SIGTERM`, or stopped when idle and started again by the next request, while a service function holds its locks finishes or rolls back and nothing is half-saved, and the first request after a start is served; a process killed in the middle of a transaction leaves nothing committed and releases its locks at once; the same Idempotency-Key retried afterwards saves once; the pool replaces a connection the server closed without an error (with `wait_timeout` 300 an idle connection stays open); a deploy that starts the app twice about 10 seconds apart loses nothing durable | H7, S11, L5 |
| T-C19 | DB2 | *New in draft 4.* Transactions: a function whose second statement times out (1205) leaves nothing behind (no number used, no row, no audit row); a deadlock is re-run up to 2 more times and then answers `JV_BUSY`; an object passed as a statement parameter is refused; a call past its 12-second deadline stops before its next statement, and the handler timer (25 seconds) never answers before the work has stopped; a connection killed during `COMMIT` answers `JV_BUSY`, and the retry with the same key returns the stored result if the transaction had committed and runs the call again if it had not; with a deliberately wrong lock order (counter first) the harness sees deadlocks (the negative control), and with the right order it sees none | S12, S8, X0 |
| T-C20 | DB2 | *New in draft 5.* Archive beside a payment: with a pause hook on the archive path (test build only, as X0 item 8, named `archive_customer.after_check`, held for less than 3 seconds), the archive of a customer whose balance, fine and advance are zero is held after its check while a `record_payment` for that customer starts; the payment waits for the customer lock, and then the archive commits and the payment is refused because the customer is archived; in the other release order the payment commits first (it leaves an advance) and the archive is refused; a customer is never archived while holding money, and with the customer-lock helper replaced by a no-op the test fails | A5, X0, K4 |
| T-C21 | DB2 | *New in draft 5.* Piece locks follow MariaDB's order of ids: two pieces whose ids sort one way in code and the other in the database (`00000001-0000-4000-8000-000000000000` and `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001`); `reissue_bill` (the old bill's piece and a new line's piece) for one customer beside `create_bill` over the same two pieces for another customer, from two connections, 40 rounds: no deadlock (1213) and no `JV_BUSY`; a build check finds no code that sorts ids before a locking statement and no locking statement for one piece inside a loop; with the lock helper replaced by a version that sorts in code and locks one piece at a time the test fails | X0, X8, X9, X10 |

### D. Security (30)
| ID | Type | What it proves | Req |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-D01 | BUILD | Every registered route has an action in the matrix or is on the public or token list, and a route registered without one fails the build; the route list with its roles is printed for review; every table has a primary key and InnoDB | A1, S1, D18 |
| T-D02 | HTTP + BUILD | **A generated test walks every row of A4 (the session calls included) and every column of `READ_COLUMNS` and `WRITE_COLUMNS` for the five callers and fails on any cell that differs from the matrix**; a caller with no session, with an ended or expired session, and a deactivated user get 401 on every route except the public list; the rights of the database user (read with `SHOW GRANTS` on the local test user built with the hPanel permission list) contain no Delete, Drop, SUPER or FILE and none of the other six unticked rights of A3; a table or function added later is reachable through no route | A3, A4 |
| T-D03 | HTTP | Staff cannot read `sku_private`, `audit_log`, bills, customers or receipts, and read only the seven settings keys of A4 (no `business`, `estimate` or `taxes`); no response to a biller or staff contains the original weight, on any read route, any function result, any bill snapshot or the CSV export | D9, D12, A4, A14 |
| T-D04 | HTTP | A biller cannot read `sku_private` or `audit_log` and cannot change stock, settings or users, and reads `daily_totals` for today and yesterday only (a longer range is `403 JV_FORBIDDEN`, O25); staff cannot change a design's material, purity or selling method | D9, A5, A4 |
| T-D05 | HTTP | Only the owner writes settings and users; the owner and a biller can add a rate, a biller only for today (yesterday's and tomorrow's are refused); the owner can correct today's rate; staff cannot; a `confirmed_by` sent by a client is refused | D4, A4 |
| T-D06 | HTTP + E2E | A user cannot change their own role or `active`; a request body, header or cookie that names a role has no effect; a deactivated user gets 401 at the next call, an open session included | D1, D2, A2, A8, A10 |
| T-D07 | BUILD + DB | No code path issues `DELETE`, `TRUNCATE` or `DROP` outside the migration runner (the statement wrapper refuses them and a grep lists every hit); on the local test user built with the hPanel permission list, `DELETE`, `DROP TABLE` and `TRUNCATE` are refused with error 1142 | S7, A3 |
| T-D08 | HTTP + BUILD | No route inserts or updates `bills`, `bill_items`, `receipts` or `receipt_allocations` directly: the route list shows none, and a `PATCH` or `PUT` to their names answers 404 | D11, D13, A4, K1 |
| T-D09 | HTTP | Function matrix over the twelve service functions (X1 to X12) and the routes of section 10, generated from the matrix for each of the five callers: no session none; staff only X8 and X9; a biller X1, X2, X3, X6 and X7 but not X4, X5, X10, X11, X12; the owner all; and the field rules (a biller's `p_cash_override_reason` is `JV_FORBIDDEN`, a past date from a biller is `JV_INVALID_INPUT`, `original_net_weight` from staff is `JV_FORBIDDEN`) | A6 |
| T-D10 | BUILD | Roles are checked in one place: a grep over the source finds no role string outside the permissions folder except a named list, and every route is registered through `route()` | A6, S10 |
| T-D11 | BUILD | The static checks of A7: the catalogue (primary keys, InnoDB, the collation `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, `DECIMAL` money columns, no float column, `RESTRICT` foreign keys, the named `CHECK`s, the list of `UNIQUE` keys equals the expected list); the `EXPLAIN` test (every locking and writing statement uses an index, and the sync comparison uses the (`updated_at`, key) index); `npm audit`; no install script in a runtime dependency; no bound money or weight parameter in SQL arithmetic, `ROUND` or a comparison without `CAST` (S3); every migration file holds only statements of the allowed list of S9; no code opens a database connection outside the pool of S11 | A7, D19, X13 |
| T-D12 | BUILD | The static build has every route; no `.map` file; none of the forbidden strings of H3; the headers of `vercel.json` answer a `curl -I` on the app host (and the content security policy names the API's origin in `connect-src` and `img-src` and no other outside origin) and the API's own headers on the API host; the environment holds only the API address | U1, U10, C10, H1 to H4 |
| T-D13 | HTTP | There is no sign-up: sign-up-looking paths answer 404; a login with an e-mail that does not exist and one with a wrong password give the same `JV_BAD_LOGIN` | U2 |
| T-D14 | DB | `audit_log` has a row, written in the same transaction, for each insert and update on the audited tables and for each service function and edit (rows counted and compared, on both mechanisms of L1, (a) and (b)), none for an update of `updated_at` alone; logins, password changes and admin calls are `event` rows, and failed logins one summary row for each hour (D24); no audit row contains a hash column, checked table by table against the exclude list of L1; an import is recorded with no actor and the role `import` (and, with triggers, a hand edit with the role `sql`; without triggers a hand edit is not audited, which is the stated gap of CMP-09); no route and no statement in the code changes or deletes it | L1, L2, D16, D23 |
| T-D15 | HTTP | The role is read from `users` on every request: a changed role takes effect at the next call; a cookie, header or body that carries a role is ignored; the cookie holds only an opaque ticket | A2 |
| T-D16 | HTTP | The internal tables (`sys_counters`, `sys_idempotency`, `sys_import_batches`, `sys_login_attempts`, `sys_job_runs`, `sys_photo_copies`, `sys_error_log` and `sessions`) and the password hash are returned by no route: a sync resource of that name answers 404 and no response contains `password_hash` | D18, D24, D25, S1 |
| T-D17 | HTTP | There is no GraphQL and no generic table route: `/graphql`, `/v1/sync/<unknown>` and a table or column name sent by a client are refused | S1, A11 |
| T-D18 | HTTP | The four derived views are readable by the owner and a biller and refused to staff and to a caller with no session | A4 |
| T-D19 | HTTP | A field outside the content columns cannot be updated (403) and an unknown field gives 422; every read role can read archived rows; an update by staff of `archived_at` on a design succeeds | A4, A5 |
| T-D20 | HTTP | The route refuses a change of one's own `role` or `active`, the owner included; the owner can change another user; a biller and staff have no route that updates users | A8 |
| T-D21 | HTTP + DB | Photos: a non-image sent as an image and a 5 MB file are refused; JPEG, PNG and WebP are recognised by their first bytes; the owner and staff can add a photo; a biller and a caller with no session cannot; only the owner can replace or remove one, and a removal sets `removed_at`, empties the bytes, keeps the row and makes the photo answer 404; an upload for a design id that does not exist yet is accepted, a repeated (photo id, kind) pair is refused with `JV_NOT_ALLOWED` while the thumbnail uploaded after the full image under one id is accepted, and the 4th photo for one design id is refused; a photo is read without a session with the immutable cache headers, an `ETag`, `nosniff` and the cross-origin resource policy of P3; no list query returns `bytes`; staff removing, reordering or replacing an id in `photo_ids` is refused while adding one is accepted; a design with an unknown id, another design's id, an id with only one kind stored or a removed id is `JV_INVALID_INPUT` | P1 to P3, D26, A5 |
| T-D22 | DB | `created_by`, `updated_by`, `set_by` and `confirmed_by` come from the session user; a client-sent value is refused as an unknown field; they are null for an import; `created_at` and `updated_at` come from the injected clock and move on every update, and a hand edit of a row moves `updated_at` through the column default when the statement does not name it (once through phpMyAdmin's own row editor, which may send the old value: the test shows what happens and the runbook rule of B5 applies) | S6, D22 |
| T-D23 | HTTP | *New in draft 4.* CORS and CSRF: a preflight from another origin gets no allow-origin header; a state-changing call without `X-JV-Client: web` is `403 JV_CSRF`; one with a wrong or missing `Origin` is `403 JV_CSRF`; no state change is reachable by GET; the origin list has no `*`; a preflight from the allowed origin for `PATCH`, `PUT` and `POST` with `X-JV-Client` and `Idempotency-Key` is accepted and a `PATCH` edit then works | A12 |
| T-D24 | HTTP | *New in draft 4; rewritten in draft 5.* Throttles: one test for each row of A13, each reaching its limit and getting 429 with `Retry-After`. Login: after 5 failures for one e-mail the waits are 1, 2, 4 and then 8 seconds and never a lock, and they survive a restart (counted in `sys_login_attempts`, D24); **50 parallel wrong passwords for one e-mail cause at most a handful of password checks a second** (at most 2 attempts for one e-mail wait or run at a time, the rest get 429 at once); **a flood of 500 unknown e-mails at once does not hold a valid login back for more than a few seconds** (at most 8 logins in flight, the rest get 429); 21 attempts in a minute from one address give a 429; with the address header removed the global cap of 60 a minute applies; the login route's handler timer is 30 seconds and a call whose client has gone does not check the password; **with the address untrusted, photo reads above the global cap of 120 a second are answered 429 and a repeated read with `If-None-Match` is answered 304 without a database read** | A13, D24 |
| T-D25 | HTTP | *New in draft 4.* Login: the cookie is `__Host-jv_session`, `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict`, with `Max-Age` of 14 days; a new ticket at every login and the old session ended; a wrong e-mail and a wrong password give the same answer in about the same time (the dummy hash runs); passwords of 9 and 129 characters, a very common password and one containing the e-mail's local part are refused; the stored hash is a self-describing Argon2id (or scrypt) string; at most 2 hashes run at once; the scrypt fallback with `maxmem` of 256 MiB hashes and verifies a password (and a `maxmem` of exactly 128 MiB fails, which is why the number is not 128) | U1, U9, U12, D24 |
| T-D26 | HTTP | *New in draft 4.* Sessions: with an injected clock a session is ended after 12 hours idle and after 14 days absolute (`401 JV_SESSION_EXPIRED`); polling does not extend it and `POST /v1/auth/activity` does; deactivating a user, changing the password and logging out end the session at once and the next call is 401 | U4, A10, C18, D24 |
| T-D27 | HTTP | *New in draft 4.* Admin door and first login: the admin routes answer 404 when `JV_ADMIN_TOKEN_HASH` is unset, refuse a wrong token and any request with an `Origin` header, send no CORS headers and are audited with actor `admin-cli`; a created user gets a random temporary password shown once and `must_change_password`; such a user can call only change-password, logout and `me` (every other call is `403 JV_PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`); changing the password ends the user's other sessions; **an admin reset ends all of that user's sessions at once and sets `must_change_password`; the admin role-change call changes a role, is audited with the actor `admin-cli`, and is the only way a role changes**; no other route can make an owner | U3, U11 |
| T-D28 | HTTP | *New in draft 4.* Nothing leaks: `JV_INTERNAL` carries no SQL or stack; data responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Request-Id`; the security headers are present; a body full of marker strings (a password, a PAN, a mobile number) posted to the API is absent from the captured log output; an unexpected error writes an `sys_error_log` row with no customer data; `/healthz` and `/readyz` answer only `{"ok":true}` or `{"ok":false}`, also when the start-up check fails for a named reason | A14, D25 |
| T-D29 | BUILD | *New in draft 4.* The API repository: no tracked file holds a URL with a password, `-----BEGIN`, a key-shaped string, `sb_secret_`, `admin123` or `staff123`; `.env` is ignored and `.env.example` holds names only; the API refuses to start when an environment variable is missing; the production bundle contains no `ctx.pause` hook (X0 item 8); the runtime dependency count is recorded | A3, U10, H3, X0 |
| T-D30 | HTTP + DB | *New in draft 5.* Bounded growth of failed logins: 20,000 failed logins for 20,000 different invented e-mails and 2,000 for one real e-mail, spread over 50 addresses, never leave more than 2,050 rows in `sys_login_attempts`, and a second run of the same load leaves the same number; with an injected clock, 24 hours of continuous attack write at most 24 `login_failures` events to `audit_log` and none for a single attempt; the throttle still answers as A13 says during the attack; the row counts of both tables after the run are printed as the worst case of D24; with the per-attempt writer of draft 4 as a negative control the test fails | A13, D24, D16, L1 |

### E. Browser (10)
| ID | Type | What it proves | Req |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-E01 | E2E | Login per role; a wrong password fails; an inactive login sees "Your account is not active"; 20 login and logout cycles never hang; the owner sees the rate card right after login; a user deactivated while signed in is logged out at the next poll; a user demoted from owner to biller while signed in loses the owner-only data (`sku_private`, the audit data) from memory within one poll, and the screen calls `me` once and does not loop on 403 | D1, A10, U1, U5 to U7, C2, C18 |
| T-E02 | E2E (two browsers) | A stock change on one shows on the other within 10 seconds; a piece sold on one shows Sold on the other; after a payment the other browser's pending and balance change | C4, C9, C17 |
| T-E03 | E2E | With the network cut a banner appears and Save is disabled; it clears after reconnect; a gateway error followed by a normal reply shows "Waking up…" and then clears, and a money call retried meanwhile with the same key saves once | C7 |
| T-E04 | E2E + UNIT | Hidden screens (Order Book, public catalogue, sample Audit and Sales tabs, the estimate date editor and edit mode, the Reports date controls and export buttons) cannot be reached; "Download everything" produces the zip, with a cell that starts with `=` written as text; `getInvoices()` returns the `Invoice` shape; every error code of S8 has a plain message and an unknown error shows the generic one | C1, C13, C15, B3 |
| T-E05 | E2E | After a session no business data is in browser storage; a failed load shows an error, never sample data; logout empties memory and stops the pollers | D20, U8, C2, C8 |
| T-E06 | E2E | A double click on Save creates one bill; a dropped response and a retry create one bill; the same bill in two tabs is saved once | L5, C5, C6 |
| T-E07 | E2E + DB2 | Polling: a row committed late by a 20-second transaction run directly on the database (an API call is cut after at most 12 seconds plus one statement of up to 8, S12) still reaches the screen; a 1,500-row poll arrives in full through paging; two rows with the same `updated_at` at a page edge are both received; a payment moves the pending of a bill on the other browser; cancelling an unpaid bill changes the other browser's outstanding within 10 seconds; a row stamped at the start of a 40-second import transaction still arrives (C4); a hidden tab polls every 30 seconds and at once when shown; a hydrate slowed to 40 seconds, while a transaction stamped before the first page was read commits 4 seconds after it, still ends with that row on the screen (C3); one poll never shows a bill without its lines (the read-only snapshot, C4) | C3, C4, D23 |
| T-E08 | E2E | The screen logs out after 12 hours without a click or key press (with a faked clock) | U4, C16 |
| T-E09 | HTTP + DRILL | The admin command creates a user, resets a password, deactivates and activates (the HTTP side is T-D27); a user who is not active reads nothing; Harsh follows the runbook once for each of the three commands | U3 |
| T-E10 | E2E + DRILL | *New in draft 5.* The real cookie: against the staging pair of names (O38, O43), in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox, a login from the website's origin sets `__Host-jv_session`, the session survives a reload, a `PATCH` edit and a `POST` with `X-JV-Client` and `Idempotency-Key` pass their preflight and work, a request from another origin gets no cookie and no allow-origin header, and photos load in `<img>` (O48 test 4); a failure re-plans the name pair or puts the API behind the website's own origin (A12) | A12, U12, P3 |

### F. Data model (10) and R. Restore (5)
| ID | Type | What it proves | Req |
|---|---|---|---|
| T-F01 | DB | Changing a design's material, purity or category changes what every piece shows | D6, D7, X8 |
| T-F02 | DB | Constraints: unique codes ignoring case, restrict foreign keys, check rules (`Sold` ties to `sold_bill_id`, `Archived` to `archived_at`), the PAN check refuses `abcde1234f`, no float column in any money path, no `data:` photo value in a text column or a JSON array, settings one row per key, every cap literal in a `CHECK` equals the constants file (read from `information_schema.CHECK_CONSTRAINTS`), and the weight rules of D7 | S3, S4, S6, D3, D7, D8, P4 |
| T-F03 | E2E + DB | 2,500 pieces all load through paging, also when the API's page cap is lowered to 500 (the cursor advances by the rows received), and the first hydrate for the M1 volumes finishes within 10 seconds on a 4G profile; 30,000 rows with random version-4 ids paged in 2-row pages come back once each, in key order (the key is compared as a UUID, C3) | C2, C3 |
| T-F04 | BUILD | All migrations on an empty database give the same schema twice, and running every file again on a half-applied schema changes nothing (DDL is not transactional); the catalogue checks of A7 pass afterwards; staging and production use the same files; every allowed form of S9 runs as the restricted user of A3, every refused form is refused by the runner and, sent directly, by MariaDB with error 1142 (`DROP TABLE IF EXISTS`, `DROP VIEW`, `RENAME TABLE`, `CREATE OR REPLACE`, `REPLACE INTO`, `TRUNCATE`), `DROP COLUMN` and `RENAME COLUMN` are refused by the runner although the database allows them, and (with the Create view box ticked on a throw-away user) a view can be created once and not replaced, which is why section 11 has no views | S9, S7, H5 |
| T-F05 | DB + HTTP | A design's `selling_method` (through `PATCH`) cannot change while it has a non-archived piece; a design with non-archived pieces cannot be archived | D6, A5 |
| T-F06 | DB | Category and subcategory names are unique ignoring case; exactly one walk-in customer exists; a mobile number that is not ten digits or `+` and 7 to 15 digits is refused, and a blank one is accepted only with an `import_batch_id`; a PAN that is not five capital letters, four digits and a capital letter is refused; `create_customer` returns the customer; the seeds hold no sample data | D5, D7, D10, X6, D20 |
| T-F07 | DB | Number formats: bill `<prefix>-<year>-<4 digits>` (and more digits after 9,999), design, piece, barcode and `OB-`; a bill saved at 23:59:59 on 31 December and one at 00:00:01 on 1 January (Asia/Kolkata) take the right year | L4 |
| T-F08 | DB + BUILD | *New in draft 4.* Connections and start-up: every checkout sets the six session settings of S11 (read back); with Hostinger's non-strict `sql_mode` an over-range amount is clamped without an error and with the API's strict mode it is refused (1264), shown side by side; the start-up check answers `503 JV_UNAVAILABLE` when the isolation level is wrong, a table is missing or the server is not MariaDB 11; a Delete or Drop right that is on is reported (amber on the dashboard), not fatal, and any right outside the ten ticked boxes of A3 is noted in the log; the pool replaces a closed connection; the pool is one pool of 8, a burst of 30 calls after a quiet spell opens at most 8 connections, a refused new connection (`connect EPERM`, simulated) is retried once after 1.1 seconds and then answered `JV_BUSY`, every checkout sets `wait_timeout` 300, and the running app opens no connection outside the pool (a source check, T-D11) | S11, S4, S8 |
| T-F09 | UNIT + HTTP | *New in draft 4.* Wire formats: money and weight are strings and a JSON number is refused; responses give money with 2 decimals and weight with 3; `created_at` and `updated_at` are UTC text with microseconds that the screen sends back unchanged for the stale check; ids are lower-case UUIDs; a business date is `YYYY-MM-DD` in Asia/Kolkata; the clock never returns the same instant twice (a thousand readings inside one microsecond, also from a fake clock fixed at one instant, are strictly increasing) | S3, S5, A5 |
| T-F10 | DB + HTTP | *New in draft 4.* PhotoStore: the same contract test (put, get, remove, sizes) passes for the in-database store and for a fake second store; `bytes` is never in a list query; the `sha256` matches the stored bytes; a 2 MiB file fits the `MEDIUMBLOB`; removal keeps the row, empties the bytes and stops serving it (the `CHECK` of D26 allows no other state); the dashboard size line goes red above 2 GB (with an injected size) **and is right against a real bulk insert**: after 400 photo rows of about 170 KB the computed size is within 15 percent of the real size of the table, where `information_schema` alone is far off (P5) | P5, P7, D26 |
| T-R01 | DRILL | The nightly encrypted export (`daily/`) and the photo copy (`photos/`) exist in the backup folder on the plan (`jv-backup/`), are still there after a redeploy, and are not reachable over HTTP; the export decrypts with the private key held by Harsh and, separately, with the one held by the owner, each proving it at drill 1 with a small test file (B4), and is readable; a failed run is visible | B2 |
| T-R02 | DRILL | Restore in the order of B4 (migrations without triggers, data in foreign-key order, each live photo row inserted together with its bytes fetched from `photos/` and checked, triggers last) from the export plus the photo copy into a throw-away MariaDB 11.8 in Docker in under 2 hours, with one photo deliberately missing from the backup folder (it is listed in the report and its row is a tombstone, so the `CHECK` of D26 holds) and a naive restore (rows first, bytes later) shown failing with error 4025: equal row counts in every table, equal photo rows and `sha256` values, 20 photos open, the settings list ticked (the unticked rights of A3, the cron job, the environment variable names, the domains), the nightly check passes, the backup folder is not reachable over HTTP and its files are fetched over SFTP with Harsh's own login; the runbook is followed by someone who did not write it | B4, B5, IM6 |
| T-R03 | DB + DRILL | The backup job's rows (`db_dump` and `photo_copy`) reach `backup_runs`, with `ok` only for a complete phase (a partial slice writes none); a photo is copied once, a second run copies nothing and records an `ok` "nothing new" run, and a size mismatch on the `stat` check is not recorded in `sys_photo_copies` (D25); the dashboard goes red after 26 hours without a good export, without a good photo copy, when the nightly check has not run for 26 hours and when the database is above 2 GB | D17, D25, B2, I12, P5 |
| T-R04 | DB + HTTP + DRILL | *New in draft 4.* The hourly tick: the nightly check runs once for a business date and not before 02:00 Asia/Kolkata; the export and the photo copy run once for a business date; a second call the same day answers "already done"; a `running` row older than 10 minutes is run again and a second tick while one runs answers "already running" (`GET_LOCK`); the endpoint refuses a missing or wrong token and any request with an `Origin` header; the tick has its own 50-second handler timer and 20-second statement limit, works in slices of about 40 seconds that the next tick continues, and writes a phase's `ok` only when it is complete; the hourly call works as the Custom cron job's `curl` command and as the GitHub workflow's HTTPS call (a DRILL looks at hPanel's list to see that the job is listed and can be deleted); `GET /internal/jobs/health` answers 503 when the last good export is older than 26 hours (and the GitHub workflow then fails); once a month for the first three months a DRILL reads the workflow's run list and the cron log to see that both callers really fired | I8, D25 |
| T-R05 | DB + E2E + DRILL | *New in draft 5.1.* Download everything: the zip holds the files of B3 and a `MANIFEST.csv` whose counts equal the database's, give or take rows made in the last two minutes; the owner-only weight is in it only for the owner; cells that start with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a carriage return are quoted; the record route accepts matching counts and stores `last_download` (one audit row, by L1), refuses counts that do not match (an empty zip, a missing file) with `JV_INVALID_INPUT` and changes nothing, and is refused to every caller but the owner; with an injected clock the dashboard card turns amber 28 days after the last download and the health line red after 35 days, and with no download at all the same happens 28 and 35 days after the first night the system ran; a new download clears both; once a month a DRILL: the owner downloads, opens the CSV files and keeps the zip outside Hostinger | B3, I12, O53 |

**Counts:** A 19, B 26, C 21, D 30, E 10, F 10, R 5: **121** (draft 5 had 120, draft 4 had 116 and draft 3 had 103). The test new in draft 5.1 is T-R05 (Download everything); the 4 new in draft 5 are T-C20 (archive beside a payment), T-C21 (piece locks in the database's id order), T-D30 (bounded growth of failed logins) and T-E10 (the real cookie and `PATCH` in three browsers); the other tests keep their IDs and many were extended (18.11 part E, 18.12). The money core is A, B and C: **66**. PLAN.md P2-T1 says "about 40 checks"; it needs updating (section 18.3).

## 16. Open items

Each item has a default, so nothing blocks the build. `[CA]` means Siddhi's chartered accountant must decide. A different answer changes **code** (not just a constant) for O1, O2, O9, O12, O22, O23 and O24 (pricing or allocation rules), for O14 (block or only warn) and for O32 (the Reports fix in M1); O38 to O53 change a domain, an account, a host or a setting (O52 changes one rule of X3, O53 adds a monthly duty of the owner), and O44 may move the API to another host; every other item changes a constant, a setting or a screen. Every `[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` marker in this spec points to an item below. There are 53 items (O1 to O53) and O10b; draft 5 changed the text or default of O11, O25, O34, O40, O43, O44, O46, O48, O49, O50 and O51 and added O52 (18.11); draft 5.1 changes O38, O43, O44, O45, O46, O47, O48 and O50 and adds O53 (18.12); O10b and O29 are closed in draft 4, and O19, O28 and O36 are reworded. **Decided by Harsh on 15 October 2026 and therefore not open:** the spec change to Node.js and MariaDB (D9 of `NODE-DESIGN.md`: approved) and photos in MariaDB behind a `PhotoStore` interface (P1, P7, D26).

| O51 | **No DELETE at all**: S7 unticks Delete and Drop on the one database user, so the housekeeping that `NODE-DESIGN.md` does with `DELETE` (old idempotency rows, expired sessions, old login attempts) is done by `UPDATE` (L6, U4, D24), and a removed photo keeps a row without bytes (P3). The reviewed design (D-Q12) keeps Delete ticked and blocks business deletes with triggers, with a second small database as the fallback | Harsh | As specified. Growth: about 150 bytes for each idempotency row, a few hundred bytes a day for sessions, failed-login counters updated in place (at most 2,050 rows, about 0.3 MB, D24) and at most 24 audit rows a day however long an attack lasts (about 5 MB a year). Migrations are create-only (S9) and views cannot be replaced, so the four derived reads are queries (section 11). The unticked boxes of A3 beyond Delete and Drop (Create view, Show view, Execute, Create routine, Alter routine, Event) are a least-privilege addition of draft 5 that Harsh may set differently. | O53 | **Monthly Download everything** (B3): the owner's reminder and the health flag. With no off-site store (O45) this download is the only copy outside Hostinger | Siddhi, Harsh | The card turns amber **28 days** after the last download and the health line turns red after **35 days**; the first download is due 28 days after the first night the system ran; the owner keeps each zip on her own computer and on a USB stick, and Harsh asks for it at his monthly check. The card and the flag are on the owner's dashboard only (I12); another interval is one number |

## 17. Out of scope, and room left for M2

**Out of scope here:** see 1.1. **Room left, so M2 needs no breaking migration:**

- **Edit a bill (BIL-08, BIL-09, DAT-02, DAT-03).** An `amend_bill` function replaces the lines with the same pricing code (R12), keeps every receipt and allocation, re-derives balances (they are derived), and keeps the old version in a `bill_revisions` table. It replaces cancel and re-issue as the usual correction (plan task P5-T4). The `bills`, `bill_items` and allocation tables need no change. When the new total is below what is already allocated, the surplus allocation must be released and Y6 applies the money again: that rule is decided when M2 is specified.
- **Order Book.** An `orders` table numbered from `order:<YYYY>`, and advances that are ordinary `money` receipts linked to an order, so they reach the ledger (LED-14).
- **Public catalogue.** Anonymous read through a public route that serves only in-stock, non-archived rows and a safe column allow-list (A4), instead of the signed-in sync routes. Never `sku_private`, never any cost or weight that is not already public. Photos are already readable by their ids (P3); a private photo mode is added then if needed (O11).
- **Returns and credit notes.** `doc_type = 'credit_note'` with a link to the original bill and the piece returned to stock; partial returns; a silver refund (O24).
- **Tax invoices.** `doc_type`, `gst_percent`, `gst_amount`, and the line `tax_rate` and `tax_amount` columns already exist; HSN and the document numbering series are added when decision 4 is made `[CA]`.
- **Staff screen.** Routes `POST /v1/users` and `PATCH /v1/users/{id}` for the owner, limited to biller and staff, with rule A8 (nobody changes their own role or `active`) tested when they are built; the admin door never reaches the browser. Until then Harsh uses the admin command (U3). Password reset by e-mail and a leaked-password lookup are later too (U9, U3).
- **Tags and scanning.** `skus.barcode` stays text, so a short numeric Code 128 value fits without a migration (plan tasks P5-T1, P5-T7).
- **Hosting and photos.** A move of the API to a small VPS if the Hostinger proof or the first months say so (O44), a move of the photos to an outside bucket behind `PhotoStore` (P7, O47), and a trigger version of the audit as a second net (O50): none needs a change to Part B.
- **Stock search and Excel upload.** Both call `create_design` (X8) or the same insert path; no schema change (plan tasks P5-T2, P5-T3).
- **A rule for one event or occasion** in the cash limit (O14), **financial-year numbering** (O31) and a **monthly sweep of photos that belong to no design** (P6).
- **Realtime** instead of polling, and a **bill-revision viewer** on the audit log (plan task P5-T8): nothing in the schema blocks them.

### 18.3 Where this spec differs from PLAN.md (to change when the plan is re-cut)

1. P1-T1 says "No open clarification marker". Markers stay, each with a default and an owner in section 16.
2. P1-T7 says "a public bucket and a private bucket". This spec has one public bucket (P1, O11).
3. P2-T1 says "six atomic database functions". This spec fixes twelve callable functions, eleven that write (X1 to X6, X8 to X12) and one read-only (X7), and "about 40 checks" becomes 62 money-core tests (103 in all in draft 3; 120 in draft 5, 18.11 part E).
4. P2-T2 says "a warning for cash of ₹2 lakh or more". This spec blocks for a biller and lets the owner pass with a reason (Z1, O14).
5. P1-T5 says "an inactivity time-out (Pro)". The Pro setting alone would not log out an open tab; U4 adds the screen's own idle logout on every plan.
6. Decision 3 says Supabase Pro from Wed 7 Oct. B1 says Pro starts before real data is entered (the date follows decision 3 and the date plan); the dev project is free.
7. P1-T9 lists four nightly checks. This spec has I1 to I7, I10 and I13, a review line (I11) and a backup heartbeat (I12).
8. P3-T1 should include the print shell (QA UI-03) as well as the 136 mm estimate sheet (UI-04).
9. The effort for P2-T1 (4.5 days) does not include X10 to X12, list prices, cash rules with an override, the mutation tests and the two-session tests; the plan step re-estimates it.
10. P2-T1 says "create a customer with an opening balance"; in this spec that is X6 (create the customer) plus X12 (add the opening balance), and the import path IM1 to IM8.
11. P1-T1 says "each of the 13 themes maps to a line and a test"; theme 13 (CMP-01, CMP-02) has none, because it is not decided here `[CA]` (section 14).
12. P2-T2 says "about 12 files" that save data; the spec counts 15 files that write data (C1 intro), and the money screens now need the dashboard line, the shared selectors and a rate entry (plan tasks FE10a, FE10b).
13. LED-09 (the Reports date range) is named by no plan task; the spec hides the Reports date controls in M1 (O32). UI-03 (tags printout) and the printed purity and date of CMP-05 are proposed for plan task P3-T1.
14. P1-T5 says the first logins are created from written steps; the spec adds the activation snippet `private.activate_user` per login (U3).

### 18.9 Clarifications made while building (orchestrator; each raised by the task named)

Gaps and one self-contradiction that an implementer found while building. None changes how a price or a balance is computed, and none is a legal point.

| Note | Raised by | Where this version resolves it |
|---|---|---|
| BN-1 | FE01 | D4 called a rate older than the bill's date "no usable rate"; R2, X1 and T-A17 say it is pre-filled and the bill saves with the warning `RATE_STALE`. D4 now agrees with R2 |
| BN-2 | FE01 | S4 now says that piroi and other charges are line amounts, so each is capped at 1,000,000,000 like the other line amounts (a `CHECK` literal and `private.limits`) |
| BN-3 | FE01 | S4 and T-A09 say that a trailing zero counts as a decimal (`scale()` of the number as sent), so the browser copy and the database refuse the same inputs |
| BN-4 | FE01 | V5 and T-B15 say that a cancelled opening-balance bill is on no sales line, cancelled or not |
| BN-5 | DB02 | S6 said the defaults of `created_at` and `updated_at` call `private.now_ts()`, which S4 forbids (a default calls built-in functions only). S6 now says the defaults are `clock_timestamp()` and the trigger `set_updated_at` stamps both from `private.now_ts()` |
| BN-6 | DB02 | D1 now states `ON DELETE RESTRICT` on the profile key, as line 107 and S7 already said for every key. DB02 had made it CASCADE so that the test harness could delete logins; the harness now deletes the profile first |
| BN-7 | DB03 | D6 left `designs.material` open. It is now `Gold` or `Silver` as a `CHECK`, so a design that could never be billed cannot exist |
| BN-8 | FE02 | The screens-by-role table of 1.3 had no row for `/scan-detail`; it now says owner only in M1, as the prototype had it (the phone menu that leads to it is M2), and a route that is not in the table is closed to everyone |
| BN-9 | DB05 | S6 said every table has `created_at` and `updated_at`; the three logs have only the time column of D16 and D17, and S6 now says so |
| BN-10 | DB05 | K5 now gives the walk-in customer a fixed id, which the seed writes (DB05 chose `00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001`) |


### 18.10 Draft 4: the move to Node.js and MariaDB (every change, section by section, with the reason)

Draft 4 changes the stack under the spec, not the money rules. Harsh decided on 15 October 2026: the frontend stays on Vercel (O39), the backend is a Node.js API on his Hostinger Business plan, the database is MariaDB 11.8 on the same plan. He approved the spec change (D9 of `NODE-DESIGN.md`) and decided that photos stay in MariaDB behind a `PhotoStore` interface (3 per design, 1000 px, quality 72). **This section is the account of draft 4 as it was written; where draft 5 or draft 5.1 changed the same line, 18.11 and 18.12 are the current word (and its "least sure" and "not verified" lists replace 18.10.5 and 18.10.6 for the changed lines).** This section is for the fresh reviewer: it lists every change so that the changed lines can be checked and the rest skipped (Playbook section 4).

**How to check.** Draft 3 is `jewelvault-app/specs/p1-foundation-and-money/spec.md` at commit `416d17a`. A byte-identical copy was committed to `jewelvault-api` by commit `c44cc2a`, so in that repository `git diff c44cc2a -- specs/p1-foundation-and-money/spec.md` is the authority; this section is only a pointer. The cross-reference lint of draft 3 (a Python script kept in the shared notes folder; for draft 3 it reported 211 defined IDs, 103 tests, none undefined, none orphaned, every marker pointed) reports **237 defined IDs and 116 tests** for draft 4 with the same result; its informational lines (requirement bullets A4, C5 and X0 have no `Test:` of their own, and two descriptive mentions of the clarification marker carry no open-item number) are the same as for draft 3. A second script compared the two texts: **147 IDs have a changed line, 39 IDs are new, none was removed**, and every one of them is named in 18.10.7 below.

**What did not change.** Every line of the money rules R2 to R11, R13, R14, K1, K3 to K6, Y1 to Y10, SF1 to SF3, V1, V2, V4, V5, I1 to I4, I7, I9, I10, I13 and Z1 to Z4 is byte-identical to draft 3, and so are D5, D8, D12, D15, D20, U7, L4, C1, C5 (a), C9 to C11, C13 to C17, B3, H2, H6, IM5 to IM7, the hazard table of section 7 and sections 18.1 to 18.9. The money rules still need no second review of their content; the reviewer's question for Part B is only whether the changed words (18.10.2, sections 8 to 13) still say the same thing.

**18.10.1 Where the inputs disagree, and which one this draft followed**

The inputs moved while this draft was written: `NODE-DESIGN.md` was read as draft 1 (890 lines) and then re-read in the version of 15 October 16:16 (948 lines), after an independent review, whose decisions DEC-1 to DEC-9 were "settled by Claude on Harsh's instruction" (only DEC-9, the spec change, and photos in MariaDB are Harsh's own); the database session added `SCHEMA.md` (commit `ce5c75a`) and Harsh's photo numbers (`DB-handover.md`) at about the same time. Draft 4 follows the reviewed `NODE-DESIGN.md` except where the table below says otherwise.

| # | Topic | `NODE-DESIGN.md` as reviewed | The database session (`PART-A-MARIADB-MAPPING.md`, `HOSTINGER-FACTS.md`, `SCHEMA.md`, `DB-handover.md`) | Draft 4 follows | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who writes the audit rows | Generated triggers if `CREATE TRIGGER` works, else the API; never both; hash columns excluded (D-Q2) | The mapping recommends triggers with the API as fallback; `SCHEMA.md` starts with the API and keeps one optional trigger file | **The behaviour is fixed** (exactly one row, hash columns excluded) **and the mechanism is chosen by the Hostinger proof**; person columns and touches are the API's in both cases | L1, L2, D16, D22, D23, O50 |
| 2 | `DELETE` | Delete stays ticked, Drop unticked; `BEFORE DELETE` triggers on business tables; fallback a second small database for the housekeeping tables (D-Q12, section 8 item 2) | One user per database; untick Delete and Drop in hPanel | **No `DELETE` at all**: housekeeping by `UPDATE`, photos by tombstone. Reasons: triggers are unproved; a second database cannot share a transaction with the money tables, and the idempotency row must | S7, L6, U4, D24, P3, O51 |
| 3 | Photos | In MariaDB (Harsh); at most 3 per design; upload first, no foreign key, insert-only rows; a session required to read; an owner-only `DELETE` route; the dump leaves `bytes` out (D-Q11) | Harsh: 3 per design, 1000 px at quality 72, thumbnail 320 px, 2 MiB; `HOSTINGER-FACTS.md`: public immutable read by id and the dump holds the photos; `SCHEMA.md`: a foreign key on `design_id` and `bytes NOT NULL` | **MariaDB with Harsh's numbers; upload first, no foreign key, insert-only** (as the design); **public read by id** with `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin` (as `HOSTINGER-FACTS.md` and O11's default; the design's session requirement is not adopted); **removal by tombstone** instead of a `DELETE` route; **the export leaves `bytes` out and a once-only photo copy goes to the bucket** (the design, because 42 retained dumps with 2 GB of photos cannot fit R2's free 10 GB) | P1 to P7, D26, B2, I12, D17, O47 |
| 4 | What starts the scheduled work | One hourly tick `POST /internal/jobs/tick` from an hPanel cron (Node.js type script or `curl`) and from GitHub Actions; the tick decides what is due; 130-second limit | Hourly, idempotent per business date | adopted | I8, D25, T-R04, O48 |
| 5 | The website host and the domain | **Cloudflare Pages** instead of Vercel (DEC-2), against Harsh's own earlier choice, and `jewelvault.harshvishwas.com` / `jewelvault-api.harshvishwas.com` (DEC-1) | (not discussed) | **Harsh later decided both** (pasted into this session): the website stays on Vercel (no Pro needed, he checked), and JewelVault gets a separate domain, not a subdomain of his own. H1 stays host-neutral; Harsh confirmed the site runs on the Hobby plan and accepted the terms risk himself (O39) | H1, A12, O38, O39 |
| 6 | An idle app | Hostinger stops idle apps; "Waking up…"; the tick keeps the app warm; a cold-start test | | adopted | H7, C7, T-E03, T-C18, O44, O48 |
| 7 | Time bounds | 15-second statement limit and 15-second deadline | the spike used a 15-second `max_statement_time` | **10-second statement limit, 15-second deadline**: 15 + 15 = 30 seconds would equal the 30-second poll overlap, 15 + 10 = 25 does not | S11, S12, C4 |
| 8 | `quote_bill` (X7) | No locks, no writes | "A pure function, needs no database call" | **Reads the piece rows and rates with plain `SELECT`s, no locks** (X7 must return list prices and warnings, which need them) | X7 |
| 9 | Restore drill target | (not specified) | a throw-away local MariaDB 11.8 in Docker | local Docker | B4, T-R02 |
| 10 | A deactivated user who types the right password | no code for it in 4.5 | (not discussed) | `403 JV_FORBIDDEN` with `details.reason = "inactive"`, shown as "Your account is not active" (draft 4's own) | U1 |
| 11 | The clock | an injected clock | (not discussed) | injected, **and it never returns the same instant twice** (draft 4's own: V3 and T-B25 need rows of one transaction to differ, which `clock_timestamp()` gave and a millisecond clock would not) | S5, V3 |
| 12 | The scrypt fallback | N=2^17, r=8, p=1 | | stated with `maxmem` raised: Node's scrypt refuses 128 × N × r above `maxmem` (default 32 MiB), and 128 MiB is needed [V] | U1 |
| 13 | The counter | (not discussed) | Spike F3 (`SPIKE-REPORT.md` section 8, found by the proof kit): create the series row before the transaction | adopted | L3, T-C01 |
| 14 | Login throttle | 5 a minute per address and e-mail when the address can be trusted, plus a progressive delay instead of a lock (the "20 an hour" rule is dropped) | | adopted | A13, T-D24 |
| 15 | The schema draft (`ce5c75a`), written in parallel and not reviewed | | It agrees with draft 4 on native `UUID`, `setting_key`, `walkin_key`, the binary PAN check, the `sys_` names, no `photo_copy` in `backup_runs` (draft 4 brings `photo_copy` back, D17), no unique key on a changing column, no trigger needed. It **differs** on: `design_photos` has a foreign key on `design_id`, `bytes NOT NULL` and no `removed_at`; `backup_runs.kind` has no `photo_copy`; `audit_log.action` allows only `insert` and `update` (draft 4 adds `event`, D16) | **Draft 4 governs**; the schema follows the spec after the review (its migrations may be edited freely until they are applied) | D16, D17, D24 to D26 |

**18.10.3 Tests: draft 3 to draft 4 (103 old IDs kept, 13 new)**

Unchanged: T-A01 to T-A08, T-A10 to T-A13, T-A16 to T-A19, T-B02 to T-B26, T-C02 to T-C04, T-C06 to T-C14, T-C16, T-C17, T-E01, T-E02, T-E04 to T-E06, T-E08, T-F01, T-F05 to T-F07. Reworded for the new mechanism, same meaning: T-A09 (a JSON number is refused), T-A14 and T-A15 (one file, two copies; version table injected), T-B01 (a direct SQL insert), T-C01 (the new-series case of spike F3), T-C05 (`Idempotency-Key`, purged results), T-C15 (lock waits and statement limits as in S11), T-D12 (the strings and headers of H1 and H3), T-E03 ("Waking up…"), T-E07 (a transaction run directly on the database), T-F03 (the API's page cap). Rewritten, the ID kept (the drill and security tests whose mechanism died):

| Test | Draft 3 proved | Draft 4 proves instead |
|---|---|---|
| `T-D01` | RLS on in every table, event trigger | every route declares its roles; every table has a primary key and InnoDB |
| `T-D02` | `anon` reads nothing, default grants | no session gives 401 everywhere; the database user has no Delete, Drop, SUPER, FILE |
| `T-D03 to T-D06, T-D18 to T-D20, T-D09` | per-role table and function rights by `set local role` | the same expectations as HTTP tests with one login per role, generated from the matrix; T-D09 adds the field rules |
| `T-D07` | no API role has `DELETE` | no code path issues `DELETE`/`TRUNCATE`/`DROP`; the hPanel permission list refuses them (error 1142) |
| `T-D08` | no direct write to bills and receipts | no such route exists |
| `T-D10` | every `security definer` has `search_path = ''` | roles are checked in one place (no role string in handlers) |
| `T-D11` | Supabase advisor | the static checks of A7 |
| `T-D13` | anonymous `signUp` fails | there is no sign-up route; unknown e-mail and wrong password look the same |
| `T-D14` | audit triggers | audit rows written in Node in the same transaction, `event` rows, `import` role |
| `T-D15` | no policy reads JWT metadata | the role is read from `users` on every request; a role in a cookie, header or body is ignored |
| `T-D16` | `private` schema grants | internal tables and the password hash are returned by no route |
| `T-D17` | `api.schemas`, GraphQL, introspection | no GraphQL and no generic table route |
| `T-D21` | Storage bucket rules | photo rules in MariaDB (first bytes, roles, tombstone, public immutable read, 3 per design) |
| `T-D22` | forced columns from `auth.uid()` | forced from the session user; clock stamps; hand edit moves `updated_at` |
| `T-E09` | `private.activate_user` | the admin command |
| `T-F02, T-F04` | caps equal `private.limits`; migrations twice | caps equal the constants file (read from `information_schema`); migrations re-runnable because DDL is not transactional |
| `T-R01 to T-R03` | R2 dump and photo copy, Supabase scratch project, two heartbeats | encrypted export without photo bytes plus a once-only photo copy, local Docker restore, two heartbeats and the 2 GB line |

New (13): T-C18 restart safety (H7); T-C19 transaction rules (S12); T-D23 CORS and CSRF (A12); T-D24 throttles (A13); T-D25 login and cookie (U1, U9, U12); T-D26 sessions (U4); T-D27 admin door and first login (U3, U11); T-D28 nothing leaks (A14); T-D29 repository and bundle (U10, H3, X0); T-F08 connections and start-up (S11); T-F09 wire formats (S3, S5); T-F10 PhotoStore (P7); T-R04 job trigger (I8).

**18.10.4 Decided, open and new**

Decided by Harsh on 15 Oct 2026, so not open: the spec change (D9) and photos in MariaDB behind `PhotoStore`. New open items, each with a default: **O38** domain (DEC-1; draft 1: D1; Harsh: a separate domain, name to come), **O39** the website host (DEC-2; draft 1: D2; Harsh: stays on Vercel, Hobby plan, risk accepted), **O40** backup key holders (DEC-4; D4), **O41** admin token and second owner (DEC-5; D5), **O42** session times (DEC-6, D6; replaces O10b), **O43** staging (DEC-7; D7), **O44** hosting risk and a VPS fallback (DEC-8; D8), **O45** off-site backup target (DEC-3, DEC-4), **O46** the plan's real limits, **O47** photo budget, **O48** the Hostinger proof, **O49** how migrations and the import reach the database, **O50** triggers and hand edits, **O51** no `DELETE`. DEC-3 of the reviewed design (photos stay in MariaDB) is Harsh's own decision and is not open.

**18.10.5 Where the reviewer should look first (the lines the writer is least sure of)**

1. **S12 (3), S11 and C4**: the whole-call bound. `max_statement_time` is per statement; fractions are accepted [W], so a stricter rule (each statement gets the time left of the 15 seconds) is possible and is a plan choice; the claim "no row is stamped more than 25 seconds before its commit" is reasoned, not run. The reviewed design keeps 15 + 15 (row 7 above).
2. **S7, L6, U4, D24, P3, O51**: no `DELETE` anywhere departs from the reviewed design (row 2). Check that nothing in the spec still needs a delete, that the growth estimate is acceptable, and that keeping an unproven trigger or a second database out of the plan is the right call.
3. **L1, O50**: the audit rule is "exactly one row, mechanism by the proof". Does CMP-09 ("records edited with no log") still count as closed when, without triggers, a hand edit in phpMyAdmin is not audited? Are the hash-column exclusions complete (`password_hash`, `token_hash`)?
4. **P1 to P7, D26, B2, I12, O47**: Harsh's photo numbers come from `DB-handover.md`, not from the design; upload first with no foreign key, insert-only rows and the `removed_at` tombstone that empties `bytes` differ from the schema draft (row 14); the public read by id with a cross-origin resource policy [U]; the export without photo bytes plus a once-only photo copy (row 3) brings `photo_copy` and its heartbeat back.
5. **H1, O39, O38, A12**: the website stays on Vercel (Hobby, risk accepted by Harsh) and the domain is a new one (Harsh's later decisions); the CSP must name the API origin in `connect-src` and `img-src`.
6. **I8, T-R04, O48**: the hourly tick; two forms of the cron start; whether the cron process sees the environment variables is unknown; the tick has no 15-second deadline.
7. **H7, C7, T-E03, T-C18**: the on-demand start of the app, "Waking up…", and how long a cold start takes are all untested [U].
8. **A13**: only the photo-read figure is the writer's own; the rest are the design's. The client address behind Hostinger's proxy is unknown.
9. **U1**: Argon2id needs Node 24 (24.7 or newer) on the plan, while Hostinger's cron uses Node 20; the scrypt fallback numbers and the 2-hash limit are from `NODE-DESIGN.md` and OWASP [N], not run.
10. **L5**: the claim-token read-back is the spike's pattern described in prose; the exact SQL sequence was not copied from the spike's helper.
11. **S5, S6**: the API clock that never repeats an instant, explicit stamps plus column defaults, and the assumption that the API and database clocks agree within seconds (C4).
12. **D10, D14, D19**: the `walkin_key` generated column is from the spike; the need for `UNIQUE (id, customer_id)` under the composite foreign keys was measured by the writer [W]; the extra internal-table indexes of D19 are the writer's own and were not run.
13. **C3, C12**: the row-comparison form is avoided for an unproven index range; the `bills` header for the biller; `pricing_sha256` in `GET /v1/auth/me` and the banner.
14. **X0 (8), T-D29**: the pause hook exists only in the test build; the production bundle must not contain it.
15. **T-B18**: mutation tests that need a dropped `CHECK` must run on a throw-away schema because MariaDB DDL cannot be rolled back.
16. **Section 0 rows 10, 13, 14, 15** are [N] or [U]: not re-read by the writer of draft 4.

**18.10.6 Not verified and not done**

The writer of draft 4 ran only the few statements marked [W] in section 0 (a throw-away local MariaDB 11.8.9 container with no volume, removed afterwards); every other MariaDB claim rests on the official documentation (section 0 rows 1 to 8, [V]) or on the spike's local run [S], and none was tried on Hostinger. Not checked at all: Hostinger's documentation pages (copied from `NODE-DESIGN.md` [N]), OWASP and MDN pages [N], the price of Vercel Pro, whether Node 24, `crypto.argon2` or TypeScript 7 exist on Hostinger's build servers, the size of the dump, network latency between the API and the database, and the plan's real limits (O46). The framework (Fastify 5), TypeScript version, Zod, the pool numbers and the task order are design and plan matters and are not fixed here. Plan task names quoted in this spec (P0-T2, P3-T1, P3-T8, FE10a, DB21, P4-T1 and the others) and the items of 18.3 that name Supabase (2, 5, 6, 14) belong to the old plan and are history until the new plan renames them. Nothing was created on Hostinger, GitHub, Vercel or Cloudflare, no credential was used, and nothing was pushed.

### 18.11 Draft 5: the answers to the two reviews of draft 4 (every finding, with the changed IDs)

Two fresh reviewers read draft 4 (the file at commit `1e954b3`) on 15 October 2026 and both said **pass with fixes, no Critical**: `spec-review-4-foundation.md` (RF4-1 to RF4-30: 3 High, 14 Medium, 13 Low) and `spec-review-4-money.md` (RM4-1 to RM4-17: 4 Medium, 13 Low), in `jewelvault-api-design/spec-draft4-review/`, with their experiments in `rm4-experiments/`. **Draft 5.1 (the same evening) changed some of the lines below again; where it did, 18.12 is current.** This section is the account of draft 5 for the third reviewer, who checks **changed lines only** (Playbook section 4). **How to check:** in `jewelvault-api`, `git diff 0f61604 -- specs/p1-foundation-and-money/spec.md` is the authority (`0f61604` is draft 4 as it stood when draft 5 began; the reviewers read `1e954b3`, and the only later changes, `c9cf6f1` and `0f61604`, are 12 lines that record Harsh's decisions on the domain and the website host, O38 and O39). This section is a pointer, and part H is generated by comparing the two files, sub-items of an ID (X0 items 1 to 8, C5 (a) and (b)) included.

**18.11 A. Totals, rulings and numbers.** **47 findings: 46 fixed, 1 fixed in part and ruled out in part (RF4-27, which Harsh's own decision of 15 October had already overtaken); none ruled out whole.** Where a reviewer gave two ways to fix something, the first (the reviewer's recommended default) was taken. The rulings of the coordinating session were applied as written: (1) the **current** `NODE-DESIGN.md` (997 lines, saved 17:27) was re-read, it was not edited, and where the spec disagrees with it the spec governs and part D lists the design lines that must change; (2) **no DELETE at the database** stays (Delete and Drop unticked), so the four derived reads are queries and not views (RM4-3, RF4-10), migrations are create-only with the allowed and the refused statements written out and tested (S9, T-F04), and a restore of the export without photo bytes does not fail the `CHECK` of D26 (B4); (3) failed-login growth is bounded by counters updated in place and one audit summary row an hour, with the worst case written down (D24, T-D30); (4) piece locks are taken by one statement in MariaDB's id order and never sorted in code (X0 item 5, T-C21), and X10 for the walk-in also refuses a silver surplus (X10, K5, T-B17). Lint (the draft-3 script, with the sub-item check added): **238 defined IDs** (draft 4: 237; the one new is O52) and **120 tests** (draft 4: 116; new: T-C20, T-C21, T-D30, T-E10), nothing undefined, nothing orphaned. **119 IDs have a changed line and 5 are new, none was removed**; every one of them is named in part B, which a script checks.

**18.11 B. Finding by finding.** "Changed" names the IDs whose line differs from draft 4; other places are named in words. Verdicts: **fixed**, or **fixed differently** (the cause is removed in another way than the reviewer wrote).

| Finding | Verdict | Changed IDs and places | What was done |
|---|---|---|---|
| RF4-1 (High) login throttle | Fixed | A13, A14, U1, D24, T-D24 | A13 now has the three caps of the current design: per e-mail (delay 1, 2, 4, at most 8 seconds, at most 2 attempts in flight for one e-mail), per address 20 a minute (global 60 when the address is missing), at most 8 logins in flight; counted when the request arrives (D24); the login route's handler timer is 30 seconds (A14); the screen never retries a login; U1 says the throttle limits the queue for the two hashes; T-D24 adds 50 parallel wrong passwords and a flood of 500 unknown e-mails. |
| RF4-2 (High) failed logins grow the database | Fixed differently | D24, D16, L1, S7, O51, B5, A13, T-D30, T-D14 | `sys_login_attempts` is now a table of counters updated in place: key (`scope`, `slot`), the slot being a salted hash of the e-mail or address modulo 1,024, so at most 2,050 rows for ever (20,000 invented e-mails filled 1,024 rows [W]); failed logins write one `audit_log` summary row for each hour, claimed by one conditional `UPDATE`, so at most 24 rows a day. The reviewer suggested a global row for unknown e-mails and one row for each real user; slots keep "unknown looks like known" and bound the table by a constant. **Worst case, by arithmetic:** draft 4 about 6 to 12 attempts a second, half a million to a million attempts and roughly 300 to 600 MB a day for ever; draft 5 about 0.3 MB fixed plus about 15 KB a day (5 MB a year). The runbook purge step is in B5 (Harsh ticks Delete for the minutes needed) and S7 names it. T-D30 is new and has the draft-4 writer as a negative control. |
| RF4-3 (High) spec follows an older design | Fixed | section 0 row 16; A13, A14, U1, D24 (login); I8, T-R04 (tick); B2, S12 (spool, snapshot closed before upload, no `SHOW CREATE TABLE`, bucket-lock limits, unencrypted photo copy); B4, T-R02 (restore order); D25, D19, T-R03 (`sys_photo_copies`); A5, P2, A4 (photo ids) | The spec was brought level with the 997-line design (the items the reviewer listed are the rows above). Row 16 now states the design's time and line count, so drift can be seen. Where the spec stays different on purpose, part D lists the design lines that must change. |
| RF4-4 (Medium) "nothing was changed" can be false | Fixed | S8, S11, S12, X0 (item 7), A14, C13, T-C19 | Numbers realigned: statement limit 8 s, deadline 12 s, so a transaction lasts at most 20 s, and the handler timer is **25 s**, always after the work has stopped (so a timer answer changed nothing). The one case nobody can know, a connection lost during `COMMIT`, is said in S8: `JV_BUSY` no longer promises "nothing was changed"; C13 words `JV_INTERNAL` and a still-failing `JV_BUSY` as "Could not confirm that this was saved." and the screen re-reads the bills first; T-C19 adds the killed-during-`COMMIT` case. |

T-E07 adds the 40-second hydrate with a 4-second commit. |

**18.11 C. Decisions waiting for Harsh or Siddhi, each with the default used** (every one is also an open item or a line the reviewer can find): (1) **O52**, the walk-in silver settlement names its bill: default **yes** (Harsh, Siddhi). (2) **O25**, the biller's cash book: default **today and yesterday only** (Siddhi). (3) **O11**, a removed photo stays in caches: default **accepted** (Siddhi). (4) **O40**, the form of the backup key: default **a file in each holder's password manager, printed only as a last resort** (Harsh, Siddhi). (5) **C13**, the wording after an unconfirmed save: default **"Could not confirm that this was saved."** with an immediate re-read (visible behaviour; Siddhi). (6) **A3, O51**, six more unticked boxes in hPanel beyond Delete and Drop (Create view, Show view, Execute, Create routine, Alter routine, Event): default **unticked**, least privilege (Harsh). (7) **A4**, the seven settings keys staff read: default as listed (Siddhi). (8) **B4**, a photo missing from the bucket at restore: default **restored as a tombstone and listed** (Harsh). (9) **O44**, an outside monitor: default **the free failing GitHub workflow of I8**; a paid vendor is Harsh's choice. (10) **O49**, a tighter second database user: not offered by Hostinger, so none. (11) **O46**, the plan expires on **22 March 2027**, before the 30 April 2027 end of the hosting period promised to Siddhi: Harsh must renew before then (a fact from hPanel in the design, not from this spec). (12) **O34**, erasure under the data-protection law against "nothing is deleted": Siddhi's CA. Decided by ruling, not open: queries instead of views, create-only migrations, no DELETE.

**18.11 D. Where this spec disagrees with `NODE-DESIGN.md` (997 lines, saved 17:27) and with the schema, and which of their lines must change.** The spec governs; neither file was edited. *Design:* 2.5 (login) keeps a `login_attempts` row for each attempt and says "counted from the `login_attempts` table": change to the counters of D24 and the hourly summary; 4.0 and 5.7 (timers): statement limit 15 s, whole call 15 s, handler timer 20 s become 8 s, 12 s and 25 s (login route 30 s and the job route 50 s stay), the tick's own statement limit is 20 s; 4.5 (`JV_INTERNAL`: "Nothing was changed") and 4.7 (`JV_BUSY`: "nothing was changed") must carry the unknown-after-`COMMIT` case and the new message of C13; 4.6 last bullet and section 8 items 2 and 3 (the API "deletes from" three housekeeping tables; Delete stays ticked; `BEFORE DELETE` triggers; a second database) become "nothing deletes, Delete and Drop unticked, purge by `UPDATE`", and D-Q12 and the login/session "deleted" wording in 2.5 and 2.7 become "revoked"; 5.4 row 3 ("ids sorted ascending in code") must read "one statement, `ORDER BY id`, no sort in code" and row 5 must read the series row first by a read then `INSERT IGNORE`, the number from the `insertId` of the `UPDATE`, never `SELECT LAST_INSERT_ID()`; 5.6 row for 1062 ("expected on the idempotency claim and when numbering skips") becomes "never expected"; 2.8 needs the role-change call and "a reset ends all sessions" is already there; 4.2 needs the first-poll rule, the read-only snapshot for a poll, the role in the poll answer, the seven staff settings keys and the biller's cash-book window, and "views" for V1 to V4 read "queries" (D-Q6); 7.3 needs the missing-photo tombstone, the read-only restore token, `GET /internal/jobs/health` and the failing workflow, and the statement that the tick does not reliably keep the app warm (Appendix C finding 1 says it does); 7.4 #1 needs the gaps of 15, 30 and 60 minutes and #3 the round-trip pass line of 30 ms and the A3 box list; 2.4 should state `maxmem` 256 MiB for the scrypt fallback; DEC-1 (the domain) and DEC-2 (Cloudflare Pages) are out of date (O38, O39) and so are the names in `API-00-STEPS-FOR-HARSH.md`. *Schema and database session (`db/`, commit `4c00b4b` and earlier, not re-read for draft 5 [N])*: any `CREATE VIEW` for V1 to V4 becomes a query file (the SQL can be reused as query text) and any invariant that reads a view reads that query; `bills.cancelled_on` and `receipts.voided_on` (DATE, present exactly when cancelled or voided) are new columns; `receipt_allocations (customer_id)` and `design_photos (design_id)` are new indexes; `sys_login_attempts` becomes (`scope`, `slot`, `window_start`, `n`) with primary key (`scope`, `slot`) and no `AUTO_INCREMENT` id or per-attempt columns; every migration must hold only the statements S9 allows (no `DROP`, `RENAME`, `CREATE OR REPLACE`; trigger files use `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS` and `CREATE TRIGGER`). `sys_photo_copies`, the `photo_copy` kind of `backup_runs`, the `event` action of `audit_log` and the tombstone columns of `design_photos` were already adopted by the database session (commit `a316651`).

**18.11 E. Tests.** The totals are **120** (A 19, B 26, C 21, D 30, E 10, F 10, R 4); the money core A, B and C is **66**. New: T-C20, T-C21, T-D30, T-E10. Changed (each is named against its finding in part B and listed in part H): the start states of T-C09 to T-C14 (RM4-4), the retyped T-C02, T-C07 and T-F05 (RF4-14), and the others as part B says. Counts and lists are produced by the lint and the comparison script, not by hand.

**18.11 F. Where the third reviewer should look first (the lines the writer is least sure of).** (1) **D24, A13, T-D30**: the slot design (two e-mails can share a counter; the one conditional `UPDATE` that claims the hourly summary; the worst-case numbers are arithmetic, not measured on Hostinger; the reviewer's per-user alternative is simpler to explain and unbounded by a constant). (2) **S11, S12, A14, X0 item 7**: the new limits 8 s, 12 s and 25 s are reasoned (12 + 8 = 20 s, inside the 30-second overlap and under the timer); nothing measured their cost on Hostinger. (3) **C3, C4**: the first-poll rule and one read-only snapshot for each poll; whether a snapshot held for a large poll is acceptable on the plan is not measured. (4) **S9, A3, T-F04**: the statement lists come from one local 11.8.9 container and the 18 boxes minus Delete and Drop; Hostinger's server may differ [U] until O48 test 3; the six extra unticked boxes go beyond the ruling. (5) **B4**: the restore order and the tombstone for a photo missing from the bucket describe a tool that does not exist yet. (6) **L3**: read first, then `INSERT IGNORE`, then the `insertId`; the year changing between the two steps is covered by one value passed to both. (7) **X0 item 3, item 8, T-C20, T-C21**: "unchanged means the same customer", the new archive pause point and the lock-order test with two UUIDs. (8) **I8**: `GET /internal/jobs/health` and the failing workflow are new surface (a token route) and GitHub's e-mail on a failed scheduled run is [N]. (9) **A13**: the global photo cap of 120 a second is the writer's own number.

**18.11 G. Not verified and not done.** The writer ran, on a throw-away local MariaDB 11.8.9 container (no volume, removed afterwards) and Node 22.22.3, and marks [W]: the list of statements allowed and refused to a user with the 18 boxes minus Delete and Drop; `START TRANSACTION READ ONLY, WITH CONSISTENT SNAPSHOT` under `REPEATABLE READ`; `ROUND` on a text parameter; the native `UUID` order; the three behaviours of `updated_at`; the stale size in `information_schema` and the right size after `ANALYZE TABLE`; the `CHECK` error 4025; the bounded counter table under 20,000 invented e-mails; and the scrypt `maxmem` limits. The reviewers' own runs (28 of 450 random sequences, 7 false `JV_BUSY`, the delayed-network runs, 256 of 4,000 products, the 86,857 audit id, the hydrate gap) are quoted as theirs and were **not** re-run. Hostinger's live pages (`node.js/overview`, `node.js/build-settings`, `websites/backups`, support articles 6976044 and 10717644) were read again on 15 October [V]; the Vercel, OWASP, MDN and R2 rows are still [N]. Nothing ran on Hostinger, in a real browser, in Fastify or in the API code, which does not exist yet. Plan task names quoted in this spec belong to the old plan. Nothing was created on or sent to Hostinger, GitHub, Vercel or Cloudflare, no credential was used, and nothing was pushed.
