### 18.12 Draft 5.1: no R2, the owner's monthly download, and the measured rules of the Hostinger proof (every change, with the reason)

Draft 5.1 was written on the evening of 15 October 2026 on the instruction of the coordinating session, which passed on Harsh's own words: "Haan pakka: R2 nahi lena, backup sirf Hostinger par, saath mein owner ka monthly Download everything. Spec draft 5.1 banwao, phir teesra review." Its four jobs were: (1) take Cloudflare R2 and the bucket locks out and say plainly what backups on the plan do and do not protect against; (2) make the owner's monthly Download everything a real requirement with a reminder, a health flag and a test; (3) write the measured rules of the Hostinger proof into the spec where they were missing and check each against the existing text; (4) list the lines of `NODE-DESIGN.md` that disagree. **How to check:** in `jewelvault-api`, `git diff e8a303b -- specs/p1-foundation-and-money/spec.md` is the authority (`e8a303b` is draft 5); this section is a pointer, and part H is generated by comparing the two files, sub-items of an ID included. **Item numbers below are those of the instruction.**

**18.12 A. Totals.** Lint (the draft-3 script): **239 defined IDs** (draft 5: 238; the one new is O53) and **121 tests** (draft 5: 120; the one new is T-R05), nothing undefined, nothing orphaned. **40 IDs have a changed line and 2 are new, none was removed**; every one is named in part B, which a script checks. Part B (money) was not touched. The grep for R2, bucket, off-site and Cloudflare finds, outside the history in sections 18.1 to 18.12, only: the places where the spec says R2 is not used (the status line, section 0 row 15 and (f), row 16, B2, O45, O48 test 6, O53), O39, H1 and A12 (Cloudflare Pages as a possible website host), P7 and section 17 (a `PhotoStore` on an outside store, a future choice), and the rate-limit "bucket" of A13.

**18.12 B. Item by item.**

| Item | Verdict | Changed IDs and places | What was done |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1. R2 and bucket locks out; backups on the plan | Fixed | B2, B4, B5, B6, D17, D25, I12, P5, O44, O45, O46, O47, O48, T-R01, T-R02, T-R03; section 0 row 15 and the "not confirmed" paragraph (f) | B2 is rewritten: the export and the photo copy go to `~/domains/<site>/jv-backup/` (the folder the database session's code uses, commit `6ec5636`), outside the build folder and the web root; layout `daily/<date>`, `monthly/<yyyy-mm>`, `photos/<photo id>-<kind>`; **create-once by linking a temporary file into place** (the same bytes again accepted, other bytes refused; `stat` replaces `HEAD`); the job prunes `daily/` after 30 days and `monthly/` after 400 and never `photos/`. B2 and O45 now say plainly **what it protects against** (a bad deploy, a damaged or wrongly edited database, a lost table) **and what it does not** (loss, suspension or expiry of the one Hostinger account, which holds the database, every export, the photo copy and Hostinger's own backup; the plan ends 22 March 2027; and there is no lock, so an attacker who controls the app can delete the backups), and that Siddhi is to be told. B4 reads the folder over SFTP with Harsh's own login (no secret of the app) and drops the locked-object drill; B6 is rewritten (all copies in one account; the owner's download is the only copy that can survive it); the fallback for an export that does not fit in slices is the Mac pull through Remote MySQL (O49), and the idea of a script run by hPanel's Node.js cron type is dropped because that job type cannot be listed or deleted. O45 is now decided (no off-site store); O48 test 6 (outbound HTTPS to the bucket) is dropped; R2 words are gone from D17, D25, P5, O47 and T-R01 to T-R03. |
| 2. The owner's monthly Download everything | Fixed | B3, D3, A4, L5, I12, section 12 dashboard line, section 1.1 (scope), section 1.3 (dashboard row), O53 (new), T-R05 (new) | B3 is a requirement: the zip (books, rates, settings, users without hashes, `MANIFEST.csv` with counts; no photos, no audit log), the record route `POST /v1/owner/download-record` (owner only; accepted only when each count matches the database's, give or take rows of the last two minutes; stored in the settings key `last_download`, which L1 audits with its one row), the dashboard card (amber from **28 days**), and the health flag (**red after 35 days**; with no download, counted from the first night the system ran). **The flag lives in the health query `readHealth`, which is I12**: it reads `settings.last_download` and the oldest `check_runs` row, so **no new table and no migration**; part E says what the database session adds. T-R05 tests it with an injected clock; O53 holds the defaults. |

| 3b. One pool of 8, at most 20 new connections a second, `EPERM` is `JV_BUSY` | Fixed | S8, S11, I8, IM1, B4, A7, T-D11, T-F08 | S11 said a pool of 10: now one pool of 8, the running app opens connections only through it (the tick's `GET_LOCK` connection is one of the 8, I8), a refused new connection is retried once after 1.1 s and then `JV_BUSY` (S8), tools and tests pace themselves to 10 new connections in 1.2 s (IM1, B4); A7 and T-D11 add a source check, T-F08 the tests. |

**18.12 C. New and changed open items, each with its default** (decisions for Harsh and Siddhi). **O38** (changed): the domain is Siddhi's own; default a new domain in her name (her cost, about ₹800 to ₹1,500 a year), or one she has after Hostinger accepts it; the message for Siddhi is written, Harsh sends it. **O45** (changed, now decided by Harsh): no off-site store; **Siddhi is to be told in plain words** that every backup the system makes is in one Hostinger account that ends on 22 March 2027, and that the monthly download is the only outside copy. **O53** (new): reminder amber at 28 days, red at 35 days, the first download due 28 days after the first night; the owner keeps each zip on her computer and a USB stick (default: Harsh asks for it at his monthly check). **O43** (changed): the build waits until Harsh has removed the two proof apps and the failed test app (`HOSTINGER-SPIKE.md` section 6) and the test database. **O48** (changed): the proof is passed (GO); the open tests are the database suite with the ten boxes of A3, the real names, GitHub deploy and the scheduled run, the database size against hPanel, the overnight idle result. **O44, O46, O47, O50** carry their new facts (the 7.4 s first request, the plan's end ending every non-downloaded backup, an outside store for photos, triggers proven).

**18.12 D. Where this spec disagrees with `NODE-DESIGN.md` (1,039 lines, saved 23:19, then going into an independent review of its own) and which of its lines must change.** The spec governs; the design was not edited. Still open from 18.11 D: the timers of 4.0 and 5.7, the wording of 4.5 and 4.7, the deletes of 4.6 and 8, the sort of ids in 5.4, the counter of 5.4, the 1062 row of 5.6, the first-poll rule in 4.2, the staff settings keys. **New in 5.1:** 2.2 and 7.4 #4 (DEC-1, `harshvishwas.com`) must name Siddhi's own domain (O38); 5.2 (the `SET SESSION` block: `max_statement_time = 15` is 8 and `wait_timeout = 120` is 300; its text already says 300 stayed alive); 7.3: (i) the paragraph "Hostinger offers a Node.js cron type, but it runs on Node 20 and nothing says it sees the app's environment" is contradicted by 7.6 (Node 24, environment loaded) and should say that only the Custom type is used, (ii) the slices bullet's fallback "the hPanel Node.js cron script doing the dump itself" becomes the Mac pull through Remote MySQL (B2), (iii) the bullets "Create-once uploads" (`If-None-Match: *`) and "A hacked API must not be able to erase the backups" (bucket locks) are dead text and become the create-once link and the plain statement of B2, (iv) restore step 3 "fetch the photo from R2" becomes the folder, (v) "`GET_LOCK` on a connection of its own" becomes one connection of the pool, (vi) the photo-copy bullet's `HEAD` becomes `stat`; 6.3, 8, 9.1 API-10 and D-Q11 where they say "R2" (the amendment at the top of 7.3 says to read it as the folder; the spec says it outright); 7.4 #6 (outbound HTTPS to the bucket) is dropped; the design has no text for B3 (the zip, the record route and its matrix row, the card and the health flag) or for `GET /internal/jobs/health`; and the design's O50 stance (D-Q2) can now say that triggers work on the plan.

**18.12 E. What the database session adds (nothing in the schema files was edited by this step).** (1) `readHealth` (`scripts/health.mjs`, I12): a `download` figure with its state, the age in days and the reference date, from `settings.last_download` (JSON `{at, by, counts}`) or, when absent, the oldest `check_runs.ran_at`; amber from 28 days, red after 35 (the reasons in dashboard order); the folder's size from `backup_runs.detail`; tests for absent, 27, 28, 35 and 36 days and for "never" counted from the first night. **No migration**: `last_download` is a row of `settings` (D3), written by the API. (2) `backup_runs.detail` carries the backup folder's size. (3) The layout of B2 (`daily/<date>`, `monthly/<yyyy-mm>`, `photos/<photo id>-<kind>`) and the pruning rule (the job deletes `daily/` after 30 days and `monthly/` after 400, never `photos/`) for the sink code. (4) The import, the restore tool and `backup.mjs` run from the Mac open at most 10 new connections in any 1.2 seconds (S11). (5) `BACKUP-RESTORE.md` "Left for the API session" says the tick has "the 130-second limit": the spec says a 50-second handler timer and slices of about 40 seconds (I8, A14); the Node.js-cron fallback is dropped (B2). (6) The audit trigger set (L1 (a)): the proof shows triggers fire, so build the generated triggers with the actor variable and the exclude list, tested on 11.8.9 and then on the plan with the ten boxes. (7) Keyset paging in any poll query compares ids as the UUID type and never aliases a column to the key's name (C3).

**18.12 F. Where the third reviewer should look first (the lines the writer is least sure of).** (1) **B3**: the rule that the API accepts the record only when the counts match "give or take the rows of the last two minutes" is the writer's; it is simple but has races, and the zip is built in the browser from a cache that may be one poll behind. (2) **B3, I12, O53**: the 28 and 35 days (35 is the instruction's default, 28 is the writer's for the reminder) and "never" counted from the first night the system ran. (3) **B2**: that `~/domains/<site>/jv-backup/` is outside the web root is assumed [U] (T-R01 checks it); the proof showed only that a folder there survives a redeploy. (4) **O38**: the default (a new domain in Siddhi's name) is the writer's reading of `MESSAGE-FOR-SIDDHI-domain.md` and of the refusal of `harshvishwas.com`; Hostinger may accept a domain she already has, and the staging website on a `hostingersite.com` address (a static site on the same plan) is an assumption of the design session that nothing here specifies. (5) **L1, O50**: "(a) is the default" rests on a proof that a trigger fires; the whole audit set and the ten-box user are untested on the plan. (6) **H7**: "the hourly tick probably keeps the process from being stopped" is an inference from gaps up to 60 minutes and the unexplained 7.4 s. (7) **I8**: the token sits in the cron command visible in hPanel (said in I8) and the 1 to 2 second timing is from one test. (8) **A12**: staging on two `hostingersite.com` addresses is cross-origin but same-site only because that suffix is not on the Public Suffix List; Safari's 7-day cap [U].

**18.12 G. Not verified and not done.** The writer read `HOSTINGER-SPIKE.md`, `API-00-RESULTS-RUNTIME.md`, `API-00-RESULTS-DB.md`, `MESSAGE-FOR-SIDDHI-domain.md`, `BACKUP-RESTORE.md` and the commit `6ec5636` (its message and the documentation, not its tests), and the changed parts of `NODE-DESIGN.md`; **it ran nothing** this time: no local database, no Hostinger, no browser. Every [P] fact is the design session's measurement through Harsh's browser, not the writer's; the file-folder create-once behaviour is the database session's tested code, not re-run. Nothing was created on or sent to Hostinger, GitHub, Vercel or Cloudflare, no credential was used, no schema file was edited, and nothing was pushed.
